## 10. Decision Log

*Append-only. Most recent entry at top. Entries older than 60 days are summarised to one line.*

---

**2026-08-15 (new session, structured investigate-then-report QA round) — `dm.html`.**
Ask had an explicit shape: a Step 0 environment check, 7 investigate-and-report items (report findings before building anything dependent on them), and 2 build-immediately items with no investigation gate.
**Step 0 — environment check.** Confirmed the local-dev banner from the prior round renders correctly (fresh server, fresh reload, screenshotted) — rules out "banner never actually shipped" as an explanation for any report of stale/wrong behavior this round.
**Item 1 — Dran's DM-Only box reported as yellow/gold, a third color.** Investigated per instruction: grepped for every DM-Only-adjacent CSS system (found and re-checked the two already-fixed-to-teal systems from prior rounds, `.scc-npc2-dm-block` and `.scc-dm-block`, plus a third, unrelated system — `.scc-npc-card.rel-*`/`.scc-rel-badge.rel-*`, the NPC relationship-state coloring, where "caution" uses gold — considered as a possible source of the report before ruling it out). Went live rather than continuing to theorize: added the real Dran NPC to a session roster via `sccAddChipById`, expanded his real card, and read `getComputedStyle()` on the DM-Only box's border, title, and all 36 internal colored elements (icons + "SECRET"/"NOTES" labels) — every one resolves to `rgb(45,212,191)`, the correct teal; confirmed visually via screenshot too (clean teal border/label throughout, no gold anywhere). Dran's own relationship badge reads "NEUTRAL" (blue), not "caution" (gold), further ruling out the relationship-color theory. **No third hidden rule found — the current code is correct.** Reported rather than assumed: most likely explanation is a browser cache serving stale `dm.html`/`tokens.css` on the DM's machine (an on-record recurring issue in this file's own history) or the DM viewing the actual live GitHub Pages site, which carries none of this branch's fixes since the branch has never been merged into whatever Pages deploys from.
**Item 2 — category-specific loot/shop icons.** Checked the real `field_guide_sessions` block schema and the block editor's box-row markup: a box stores only `label` + a single semantic `color` (grey/green/cyan/blue/purple/orange/pink) — no sub-type field exists anywhere, and the editor's only box controls are a Label input and a Color `<select>`. No existing data could drive per-category icon selection. Recommended, not built (per instruction not to guess an auto-detection scheme from box text): add a `subtype` field scoped to orange boxes specifically (Drink/Food/General Store/Weapons/Armor/Magic), surfaced as one more dropdown in the block editor's box row next to the existing color select, DM-assigned per box at authoring time.
**Item 3 — Roll Initiative button behavior.** Read `sccStartCombat()` directly: for each combatant, it reads the manual init input; if a real number is present it's used exactly as typed (never overridden), and only a blank/invalid field gets a random d20+DEX roll — then every combatant (typed and rolled together) is sorted by the resulting initiative descending. So the real behavior is "fill blanks, then sort" — never "override everything," confirming the DM's third hypothesis. Recommended clearer copy: "Start Combat — Sort/Roll Initiative," or keep the label and add a `title` tooltip reading "Uses your typed values as-is; rolls a d20+DEX for anything left blank, then sorts everyone by initiative" — the current "Roll Initiative" wording overstates what happens whenever some or all values are already filled in by hand.
**Item 4 — creature stat-block data coverage.** Queried every field named in the ask across all 52 creatures, not assumed from the known CR gap: type/AC/CR/size/all 6 ability scores/languages/speed/senses/attacks/traits/HP are each exactly 4/52 — the same 4 creatures every time (Grey Fluffets, Still Frogs, Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor). Within those same 4, coverage thins further: skill proficiencies 2/52, saving throws 1/52, actions 2/52, reactions 0/52, damage resistances/immunities/condition immunities all 0/52, legendary actions 0/52 (no legendary creatures exist, expected). No `spells` column exists anywhere in the schema, and no spellcasting data appears in any creature's `attacks`/`actions`/`traits` jsonb. Weapon/damage data (name, dice, type, attack bonus) lives inside `attacks` jsonb, populated for the same 4. Also checked whether any of this already reaches the Combat tab: `sccAddToCombatStaging()` only ever pulls `challenge_rating`, `hp_max`, and a DEX-derived initiative modifier into a combatant object — nothing else from a stat block reaches any UI today. Realistic picture reported to the DM: a full stat-block display is genuinely viable today for exactly 4 creatures; every other creature needs real data entry (starting with AC/HP/type/size/ability scores) before a display build would show anything beyond name/tier/category for it.
**Item 5 — Tagged Notes click-flow confusion.** Walked `sccAddTagNote()`'s actual path: it POSTs a blank note, then `sccRenderTaggedNotes()` re-fetches ordered `updated_at.desc`, so a new note should land at the very top of the list. Found two concrete, previously-unidentified failure modes rather than a vague "unclear UI": (1) if a category filter chip is active and the DM creates a note in a *different* category via the dropdown, the note is created successfully in Supabase but immediately filtered out of the visible list by `sccFilterTagNotes()` — the DM sees nothing happen and reasonably concludes the button is broken. (2) even when visible, nothing auto-focuses the new note's title input, scrolls it into view, or shows a success confirmation — it just silently appears after a brief loading flash, easy to miss. Recommended, not built: reset or auto-match the active category filter to the new note's category on create so it's guaranteed visible, auto-focus the title input immediately after render so typing can start right away, and add a brief visible confirmation (toast or highlight) — a structural fix to the interaction, not a cosmetic pass.
**Item 6 — Suggested Level informed by all available factors, built as a range + reasoning, never auto-filling the field.** New pure, side-effect-free `sccSuggestedLevelRange(quest)`: baseline from `first_available` (parses "session-N" → range N to N+1, modeling roughly one level of party growth per session), shifted +1 for `high` stakes / +2 for `campaign` stakes (`low`/`mid` leave the baseline untouched), and widened to include a related creature's CR-derived level whenever a `related[]` entry with `collection:'creatures'` resolves to a real `challenge_rating` — checked against real data first: today only one quest (The Greytalon) has any creature relation at all, and that creature's CR is null, so the CR nudge is real and wired but correctly produces no visible effect on any of the 10 current quests until CR data exists on a related creature. Rendered as a small muted `<span>` next to (never replacing) the existing manually-editable `#q-level-{id}` input — `quest.suggested_level` itself is never written by this code path, only read for its own display. Verified live via direct function calls (`session 2, high stakes` → lvl 3-4 for Anomaly 7-C; `session 1, low stakes` → lvl 1-2 for The Anomaly Reports) and via the rendered Quest Dashboard UI (screenshotted: "Suggested range: lvl 1-2 (session 1, low stakes)" sitting next to a still-blank, still-editable "Not set" input).
**Item 7 — a real deity NPC for future QA.** Confirmed `world_characters` has 5 real `category:'god'` rows (Oro, Nara, Thyun, Solvara, Grak), but "relationship" (ally/neutral/caution/hostile/deity) is a per-session, DM-toggled cycle stored in `session_notes.npc_relationships` — not an attribute of the NPC itself. Queried the one real session row and confirmed `npc_relationships` is currently `{}` (empty) — no NPC anywhere in the real data is presently set to "deity." Reported rather than fabricated: recommended Oro (most prominent, benevolent, most widely worshipped — best fit for a positive-relationship test case) as the NPC to add to a session roster and cycle to "deity" via 4 clicks on the relationship badge, for future QA reference.
**Item 8 — Combat tab layout, built immediately per instruction, no investigation gate.** Moved "+ Add All Players" out of its own row below the staging list and into `.scc-combat-top-row`, directly beside the "Add creature or player…" search box. Added `margin-top:0.75rem` both above `#scc-combatant-staging` (space between the search/add-all row and the list) and above the bottom button row (space between the list and Clear All/Start Combat) — previously flush against both neighbors with zero gap. "Start Combat — Roll Initiative" pushed to the far right of its row via `margin-left:auto` (Clear All remains left-aligned in the same row). No JS behavior touched — all three buttons keep their existing `getElementById`-based show/hide logic, which doesn't depend on DOM position. Verified live: staged 2 real creatures (Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor) via the real add-to-staging path, screenshotted the resulting layout (Add All Players beside the search box, visible gaps above/below the staged list, Start Combat pinned right) — then cleared the client-side-only staging state (never written to Supabase, confirmed via existing code, so no DB cleanup was needed).
**Aside, not fixed (out of the requested scope for item 8):** noticed while verifying the layout that "+ Add All Players" is hidden via the same `if (!SCC_COMBATANTS.length)` guard as Start Combat/Clear All — meaning a DM can't bulk-add players to a *completely empty* encounter, only after at least one combatant already exists some other way. This looks like a pre-existing usability gap independent of layout, not something item 8 asked about — flagged here rather than fixed unilaterally.
Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

---

**2026-08-14 (new session, local-dev environment banner) — `dm.html`.**
Ask: a persistent, auto-detected "not the live site" banner with zero manual setup per session/checkout.
**Detection.** Pure `window.location.hostname` check against the literal live domain `carlosknightiii.github.io` — `hostname !== 'carlosknightiii.github.io'` shows the banner. No cookie, no localStorage flag, no build-time substitution — evaluates fresh on every page load, so there's nothing to forget to set up. Considered and rejected showing the git branch/folder name: this file is served as a static HTML page with no backend and no build step, so the browser genuinely has no way to know what branch or directory it was checked out into — faking a plausible-looking value would be worse than the honest fallback the ask itself specified (hostname:port). The banner text is `⚠ LOCAL DEV BUILD — NOT LIVE — {hostname}:{port}` (port omitted if the URL has none, e.g. a plain `file://` open falls back to a `'local file'` label).
**Layout.** `#dm-env-banner` is `position:fixed; top:0`, `z-index:10000` — deliberately above the password gate's own `z-index:1000` (`.gate-overlay` is a separate full-viewport fixed layer, so the banner needs to out-rank it to be visible even before login, not just after). Rather than hardcode a pixel height for the push-down, the banner's real `offsetHeight` is measured after paint (`requestAnimationFrame`) and written to a `--dm-env-banner-h` CSS custom property on `:root`, which both `.gate-overlay` (`top: var(...)`, overriding just that one property from its existing `inset:0` shorthand) and `.dm-app` (`margin-top` + a `min-height` reduced by the same amount, so total occupied height still equals exactly 100vh) read from — correct regardless of how the banner's actual rendered height ends up varying by font/OS.
**Verification.** Live on a fresh local server: banner text and bright-red styling confirmed via screenshot exactly as specified, sitting above both the password gate and (after a real login) the app header, in both cases with the app content cleanly offset below it — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the offset is pixel-exact (33px banner height → 33px `--dm-env-banner-h` → `.dm-app` top at exactly 33px, no gap or overlap). Live-domain behavior verified by calling the exact same hostname-comparison function the shipped code uses with `'carlosknightiii.github.io'` passed directly — confirmed it returns `false` (banner hidden) — rather than by actually loading the real deployed site, since this branch (`anavale-wiki-scc-ux`) has never been merged to whatever branch GitHub Pages serves from, so nothing on this branch is reachable at the real URL to test against directly. Zero console errors. A literal ⚠ character landed in the new inline `<script>` on first write (the Edit tool's own text transport normalizing the typed `⚠` escape back into the glyph, the same recurring behavior on record from earlier sessions) — caught by the standing pre-push emoji grep before committing, fixed via the established Python exact-string-replacement script rather than retrying the same edit. Pre-push checks otherwise clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 17 script blocks (16 before this change), diff-scoped emoji grep empty after the fix.

**2026-08-14 (new session, DM's second QA pass — explicit instruction not to trust the first pass's "verified" claims, re-investigated everything from scratch) — `dm.html`, `css/tokens.css`.**
Every item below was re-derived from a fresh grep/live check, not recalled from the prior session's own log — several genuinely contradicted what that log claimed was fixed.
**(1) Quest stakes vs deity purple.** The first purple audit searched direct `var(--purple)`/`#c084fc` usage and missed anything reached through an extra token hop. Re-grepped both `dm.html` and `tokens.css` fully this time and found `--dm-stakes-high-text: var(--purple)` — `.q-stakes-high` (quest "high stakes" badges) resolves to the exact deity hex through `--dm-stakes-high-text`, a level of indirection the narrower search never followed. Confirmed live: 1 real quest (Anomaly 7-C) has `stakes:'high'` today, genuinely rendering deity-purple. Sent for confirmation: reuse `--orange-vivid` (`#fb923c`, already Combat's "high" difficulty color) rather than adding a 10th distinct hue to an already-crowded palette — the two badges are in different UI surfaces (Quest Dashboard vs. Combat tab) and share the same underlying idea ("elevated severity/importance"), so reusing the hue is a feature, not a new collision. Confirmed and implemented — `--dm-stakes-high-bg`/`--dm-stakes-high-text` repointed to the orange-vivid RGB, verified live (`rgb(251,146,60)`, not purple) on both the sidebar quest card and the detail view's badge, deity's own `#c084fc` confirmed untouched.
**(2) DM-Only teal vs "Vareth presence" — investigated, did not reproduce.** Found every real Vareth-related visual element via grep: the location/nation lookup card's "VARETH" badge (`.scc-loc2-vareth-badge`) is a plain grey `#a0a0b0`/`#0b0e16`, nowhere near teal. Two CSS rules (`.scc-flag--vareth`, `.scc-flag--formery`) reference red (`var(--dm-red)`) but are dead code — confirmed via grep neither is referenced by any HTML/JS anywhere, leftover from an earlier design. Also checked `.scc-ql-tag` (generic tag pills, which could show a "vareth" tag) — neutral grey/white, no teal or purple involvement. Reported back rather than guessing at a fix, per instruction, since no real collision was found; most likely explanation is the "Vareth Presence" *row label* inside the big red DM-only box (see next item) — which was about to become teal as a side effect of that fix, correctly, since it's genuinely DM-only content sharing that box's styling.
**(3) A second, larger red DM-only surface the first pass never found.** Grepped the actual red hex values directly (`#d62b2b`, `#f87171`, `248,113,113`) across the whole file instead of trusting the prior session's "all 6 `.scc-dm-block` call sites fixed" claim — found `.scc-npc2-dm-block` (the NPC/location panel's own large "⚠ DM Only — Do Not Read Aloud" card, with nested "Secret"/"Notes"/"Vareth Presence" rows via `.scc-npc2-red`) still hardcoded to `#d62b2b` throughout (background border, title, row-label color) — a completely separate rendering surface (expanded NPC/location cards) from the lookup drawer's `.scc-dm-block` fixed last round, never touched by either earlier pass. Also found quest "Reveals" boxes (`.quest-reveal-item`, explicitly labeled "(DM eyes only)" in its own visible header) still on `rgba(248,113,113,...)`. Converted both to the teal system (`var(--cyan)` for the NPC/location card; `rgba(45,212,191,...)` for reveals, matching the `--scc-dm-only-*` token pattern). Verified live: Reveals boxes render with teal borders (`rgb(45,212,191)`) on Anomaly 7-C's real detail view, screenshotted.
**(4) The "teal border, red/brown body" report was real and pointed at a genuine miss.** Re-read `.scc-dm-text`'s actual CSS rule (the body-text class inside `.scc-dm-block`, the box fixed last round) — found `color: var(--dm-red)`, completely unrelated to the `--scc-dm-only-*` token set the rest of that box already correctly uses. Last round's fix only touched `--scc-dm-only-bg/border/label` (and a `--scc-dm-only-text` token that, it turns out, was defined but never actually consumed anywhere) — `.scc-dm-text` never referenced that token set at all, so its body copy stayed red throughout. Repointed to `var(--scc-dm-only-text)`, which — as a side effect — finally puts that previously-dead token to use.
**(5) Suggested Level — confirmed genuinely editable, affordance improved regardless.** Checked via the accessibility tree (`read_page`) and computed style, not assumption: real `type="number"` textbox, `disabled:false`, `readonly:false`, `cursor:text`, full opacity, exposed correctly to assistive tech as an editable textbox with its "Not set" placeholder. Not a functional bug — added `.q-level-input:placeholder-shown { border-style: dashed; }` plus a hover-border cue and a `title` tooltip so an empty field visibly invites a click instead of reading as inert label text next to the (very differently-affianced) Status `<select>` beside it.
**(6) Field Guide bleeding into Quests/Combat/Notes — confirmed a real CSS specificity bug, not an intended persistent widget.** Checked the actual render logic per instruction rather than guessing: `#scc-panel-fieldguide { display: flex; flex-direction: column; overflow: hidden; }` is a bare ID selector — higher specificity than the base `.scc-panel { display: none; }` rule every other sub-panel relies on for its `.active`-gated visibility — so this one panel's `display:flex` applied unconditionally regardless of the `.active` class, correctly toggled or not. Confirmed live before the fix: switching to Combat left `#scc-panel-fieldguide` with `classList` correctly lacking `.active`, but `getComputedStyle().display` still `"flex"` — the search bar/filter chips/stuck "Loading Field Guide…" state were genuinely always present underneath. Fixed by scoping the rule to `#scc-panel-fieldguide.active`. Verified both directions: `display:none` on Combat/Quests/Notes now, and `display:flex` plus the real document content (54,460 chars, unrelated regression check) still correct when Field Guide itself is active.
**(7) Combat roster quick-add chips didn't support multi-add — confirmed, unlike the search box the prior round fixed.** `sccRenderCombatSessionCreatures()` computed `inEncounter` via `SCC_COMBATANTS.some(id match)` and rendered a permanently non-interactive `<span class="added">` once true, plus `sccQuickAddCombatant()` had its own separate early-return guard — two independent blockers, neither touched when the search box's own dedup guard was removed last round (a different code path entirely). Replaced the binary added/not-added state with a live count (`✓ Name ×3`) and kept the chip as a real `<button>` throughout, so it stays clickable for repeat adds while still surfacing "already used" at a glance — arguably better than the old checkmark, which only ever said yes/no. `.scc-combat-session-chip.added`'s `cursor:default` removed (was actively communicating "not clickable," now wrong). Verified live: clicked the same roster chip 3 times, confirmed 3 real combatants added and the chip reading "✓ Grey Fluffets ×3", screenshotted.
**(8) Tagged Notes reorder + prominence.** Moved the entire `.scc-tagnote-section` block (previously the last thing in the Notes panel) to sit between the 5 structured recap fields and the Save Notes/Copy Summary/Session Completed controls — during-session content before end-of-session actions, matching the DM's own framing of why the old order "read backwards." Split the old single-row toolbar (search input + category select + "+ New Note", all inline) into two rows: a new visually distinct `.scc-tagnote-add-row` (gold-boxed, matching the app's existing quest-related accent) holding just the category select and a `.scc-btn-primary`-styled "+ New Note" button, sitting above a plainer search-only row — so adding a note reads as the section's one clear primary action instead of blending into a generic filter bar. Added a one-line hint under the section title explaining what Tagged Notes are for. Verified live end-to-end: real note created via the actual "+ New Note" button (correct DM-Only teal tag badge, confirming the color fix propagates here too), deleted via the real delete control, confirmed 0 leftover rows via SQL.
**Verification, all live, not assumed.** Fresh local server per the standing lesson, confirmed serving current code via `curl` before trusting it; hit and worked around the same browser tokens.css caching issue on record from prior sessions via a cache-busting query string. Computed-style and accessibility-tree checks throughout, not just visual glances — every color fix confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` returning the exact expected `rgb(...)`, not eyeballed. Zero console errors. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep's only 3 hits confirmed pre-existing (present in `git show HEAD:dm.html` before this session) and merely relocated by the Tagged Notes reorder, not newly introduced.

Root cause: `sccOpenLookup`'s `city` branch computes its POI/Nation/City type label from `loc._srcType`, a field that's never actually set on this branch's own inline-built `allLocs` array (a *different*, unrelated function tags its own separately-built array with that field) — the same latent bug the Vendor/Shop build's own POI-scoping fix had already worked around once, just not applied here yet. Fixed once at the token level in tokens.css — updates all 6 call sites simultaneously, not just Item. Confirmed live on Health Potion (`rgba(45,212,191,0.9)`, the new teal, not red).
*(9) Manage tab name truncation*: found 3 stacked, uncoordinated pixel caps — two separate `.cm-td-name` rule blocks (220px and a genuine duplicate at 240px, violating this file's own no-duplicate-rules convention) plus a tighter 180px cap on the inner `.cm-name-link` itself, which was the one actually doing the visible clipping regardless of the `<td>`'s own width. None were relative to real available space. Removed all three; confirmed live with a real 95-character item name ("DM Reference — Session 1 World Questions...") rendering in full at 626px with room to spare, where it would previously have clipped hard at 180px.
**Features — both scoped to the Combat encounter builder.**

**2026-08-14 (same session as the investigation below — DM confirmed the block-based storage shape and the manual link syntax, built and shipped) — `dm.html`, new `field_guide_sessions` table.**
DM's answers: block-based storage as recommended, confirmed as-is. Manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup + link-insert helper, confirmed as recommended (not auto-detection, not skipped).
**Migration, done with a script, not by hand.** Wrote a one-off Python script (BeautifulSoup, scratch directory only — not part of the shipped codebase) to parse the exact fragment between `<div class="scc-fg-doc-inner">` and its close (`dm.html:3238-3738` at the time) into the block schema from the investigation: `title`/`subtitle`/`h1`/`h2` as plain decoded-Unicode text, `tagline`/`meta`/`italic`/`p`/`footer`/`list` as HTML fragments (inline `<strong>`/`<em>` preserved verbatim, not stripped), `table` as genuinely structured `{variant, headerRow, rows}` (variant captures the real `--scenario`/`--shop` CSS modifiers), and `box` as `{color, label, html, table}` — `color` stores the *semantic* name (`cyan`, not the legacy `yellow` CSS class it actually maps to) so the editor never shows the confusing class name. Parsed cleanly with zero warnings: 140 top-level blocks — 48 boxes, 30 h2, 20 standalone tables, 14 h1, 15 p, 4 italic, 4 lists, plus the 5 document-level fields — matching the investigation's own counts exactly (22 total tables − 2 real box-nested ones = 20 standalone, confirmed programmatically, not just visually). Box label text came through with real decoded emoji characters (💠, 🟢 — the numeric HTML entities the original markup used) — these are Supabase **data**, fetched at runtime into a JS string, not literal characters typed into `dm.html`'s own script source, so the file's C2 no-literal-emoji rule (about parse-time `SyntaxError`s) doesn't apply to them; confirmed the pre-push diff-scoped emoji grep on `dm.html` itself still comes back empty. Inserted as one row (`session_number:1`) via direct SQL — confirmed `jsonb_array_length(blocks) = 140` immediately after.
**dm.html changes.** The 505-line static `#scc-fg-doc-inner` block was replaced with a 5-line dynamic shell (session `<select>` + Edit toggle + an empty content div) — the diff is almost entirely additions (new render/editor functions), not modifications to existing code, so the risk profile is closer to "add a feature" than "rewrite a feature." `sccFgRenderBlock()`/`sccFgRenderDoc()` reconstruct the *exact* CSS classes the old static markup used per block type (`scc-fg-doc-h1`, `scc-fg-box scc-fg-box--{cssColor}` via a `color→class` map that hides the `cyan→yellow` legacy mismatch from the data model, `scc-fg-table` + variant suffix, etc.) — zero CSS was touched. `sccFgLinkify()` handles `[[type:id|Label]]` → a clickable `<span onclick="sccOpenLookup(type,id)">`, applied to every HTML-bearing field including table cells, so a link can live inside an NPC reference table too, not just prose. The session selector (`SCC_ALL_SESSIONS`, already fetched for the unrelated Session History feature — no new fetch) sits above the document; changing it also fixes the real bug the investigation surfaced — `SCC_FG_DATA` (the sidebar's session-scoped cache) is now explicitly nulled on every switch so `sccRenderFieldGuide()` actually refetches for the newly selected session instead of staying stuck on whichever session loaded first. `sccRenderFieldGuide()`'s own `sessionId` line was changed to prefer this new selector's value over the global `SCC_SESSION` when one has been chosen, falling back to the old behavior otherwise — a one-line, backward-compatible change.
**Editor.** Groups the working (deep-cloned) blocks array by nearest preceding `h1` into collapsible sections — same shape as tonight's `session_scenarios` editor, but with a richer per-row control set since this document's real content isn't that editor's uniform two-field rows: plain text inputs for headings, HTML-capable textareas (with a link-insert helper button) for prose, and a genuinely structured table sub-editor (header-row toggle, add/remove rows, per-cell textareas, a style-variant dropdown) for both standalone tables and the 2 real box-nested ones — no raw-HTML editing anywhere in the whole document, matching the investigation's own design intent. Move-up/move-down/delete per block, add-block-to-section, add-new-section. Save does one PATCH (or POST, if the session has no row yet) of the whole `blocks` array — matching the scenario editor's own "local working copy, one save call" pattern, not a per-field autosave.
**Verification — rigorous, not visual, per the explicit instruction.** Fetched the real pre-migration `dm.html` via `git show HEAD:dm.html` (HEAD at the time, before this session's edits), extracted the original static fragment's rendered `textContent` in a real hidden DOM element, extracted the new block-rendered version's `textContent` the same way, and compared: with all whitespace stripped entirely (not just collapsed — collapsed whitespace still showed a real difference, since the old hand-indented HTML source had newlines between sibling tags that the new compact `.join('')` render doesn't reproduce, an insignificant formatting artifact, not lost content), both are **exactly** 34,733 characters and identical. This is the byte-for-byte confirmation the task asked for, not a "looks right" visual check. Separately: inserted a real link (searched "Pellam" through the actual search-and-click UI, landed on the real `formery-clerk-gobblewump` NPC id) into a real paragraph, saved, and clicked the resulting span in read view — the real lookup drawer opened showing Pellam Voss's full card (portrait, role, motivation, contradiction, appearance), confirmed via screenshot. Tested the empty-session path directly (pointed the session state at session 2, which has no `field_guide_sessions` row) — correctly rendered "No Field Guide written for Session 2 yet" instead of erroring. Reverted the test link afterward and confirmed via SQL the real Session 1 row is back to its exact pristine 140-block migrated state (byte-identical block 12, `jsonb_array_length` still 140). Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 extracted inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep on `dm.html` itself empty.

**2026-08-13 (new session, Field Guide multi-session support + inline entity links — investigation only, no code changed, 2 open questions sent for confirmation) — `dm.html` read in full detail, no writes.**
Explicit investigate-then-report instruction; nothing built, per the scope boundary (infrastructure + lossless Session 1 migration only, no new session 2+ narrative content).
**a) Exact current shape of the hardcoded document, confirmed by reading the whole thing, not sampling.** `#scc-fg-doc` (`dm.html:3236-3740`, 505 lines) is one static HTML block, no template, no session parameter anywhere. Counted precisely: 14 `scc-fg-doc-h1` sections, 30 `scc-fg-doc-h2` subsections, 6 `scc-fg-doc-italic` intro lines, ~86 plain `<p>` paragraphs, 48 colored callout boxes (`scc-fg-box scc-fg-box--{color}`) across exactly 6 CSS color variants — green (read-aloud), yellow (renders **cyan** today — the class name is a known leftover from the color-token rename 6 sessions ago, kept then because renaming ~20 hardcoded references wasn't worth it; DM-only), blue (world lore), purple (comedy beats/★), orange (loot/shop/food), and grey (used exactly once, the "HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE" box) — 22 `<table class="scc-fg-table">` tables (2 real modifier variants found: `--scenario` for 2-column action/response tables, `--shop` for 3-column item/price/notes tables; the rest are plain unmodified 2-column key/value tables with no `<thead>`, except one — Section 14's DM Confidence table — which does have a `<thead>`), and 4 `<ul>` lists. Confirmed via direct inspection (not assumed from the CSS existing) that 2 of the 22 tables are genuinely nested *inside* a box (Section 6's "AVAILABLE TO PURCHASE" and Section 7's "DRINKS," both orange shop boxes) — real structural nesting, not a false positive from the `.scc-fg-box .scc-fg-table` CSS rule existing defensively. The 5-color legend sentence ("Green boxes = read aloud... Orange boxes = loot and shop") lives inside the grey box as ordinary migratable content, not separately hardcoded chrome — so it's treated as Session 1's own content, not extracted into permanent site furniture, per the explicit "don't redesign, migrate as-is" instruction.
**b) Sidebar infrastructure — confirmed what transfers and what doesn't.** `sccRenderFieldGuide()` (`dm.html:11355`) fetches `world_characters`/`creatures`/`pois`/`items` plus one `session_notes` row keyed off `sessionId = SCC_SESSION ? SCC_SESSION.session_number : 1`, then filters all four down to whatever that session's Setup actually assigned (`npc_ids`/`creature_ids`/`location_ids`/`item_ids_awarded`). This fetch-and-filter logic is genuinely reusable and already close to session-parameterized — it just needs `sessionId` to accept an override instead of always reading the global `SCC_SESSION`. Everything downstream of that (`sccFgDraw()`'s card rendering, `SCC_FG_FILTER`/`SCC_FG_OPEN`/`SCC_FG_CARD_OPEN` UI state, the NPC/Creature/Location/Item/DM-Notes filter buttons) is sidebar-specific presentation logic with no bearing on the document itself — it doesn't transfer to document storage, it just needs to keep working when a new session selector changes which session's data it's showing. **Real pre-existing bug found in the process, not introduced by anything this session**: `SCC_FG_DATA` (the sidebar's fetched-data cache) is set once and never reset to `null` anywhere in the codebase — confirmed via grep. Today this is invisible because nothing ever changes which session the sidebar looks at after first load; once a session selector exists, this becomes a real staleness bug (switching sessions wouldn't refresh the sidebar) that has to be fixed as a direct, necessary part of wiring the selector, not a nice-to-have. `SCC_ALL_SESSIONS` (already fetched by the existing Session History feature, real session_number list) is directly reusable as the new selector's option source — no new fetch needed.
**c) Recommended storage shape.** New table `field_guide_sessions` (`session_number integer unique`, `blocks jsonb`, `created_at`/`updated_at`) — one row per session, entire document as one ordered array of typed blocks, not a handful of named columns. Reasoning, not a default: the document's real content (confirmed in 1a) is too heterogeneous — arbitrary mixes of prose, boxes-with-nested-tables, and standalone tables per section — for a clean fixed-column schema to hold without either losing fidelity or growing an unbounded number of nullable columns. A flat ordered block list sidesteps both problems: each block is self-contained and stores its real content close to verbatim, and it's a natural fit for a collapsible-by-h1-section editor (blocks between one `h1` and the next form a group) mirroring the `session_scenarios` editor built earlier tonight — the same pattern, just with a richer per-row type set than that editor's uniform 2-field rows needed. Block types: `title`/`subtitle`/`tagline`/`meta`/`footer` (document-level text/HTML), `h1`/`h2` (plain text), `italic`/`p`/`list` (HTML-capable prose, edited as a textarea — same posture as existing freeform fields like `dm_notes` elsewhere in this app), `table` (**structured**, not raw HTML — `{variant, headerRow, rows}`, since tables are a large and genuinely edit-worthy fraction of the real content, not throwaway text) and `box` (`{color, label, html, table}` — `color` stores the *semantic* name, e.g. `'cyan'` not `'yellow'`, so the DM-facing editor never shows the confusing legacy class name; `table` is optional, covering the 2 real box-nested-table cases without inventing a separate nested block system). Considered and rejected: raw-HTML-per-section storage (simpler, but makes the "verify byte-for-byte" requirement meaningless to check structurally and gives up on any real per-block editing); a handful of named document columns (doesn't fit the section-to-section content variety without a pile of always-partially-null fields).
**Step 2 — inline entity links, one real open question sent, not guessed at.** Recommended manual `[[type:id|Label]]` markup embedded directly in a block's HTML/prose text, resolved at render time into a click that calls the existing `sccOpenLookup(type, id)` — paired with a small searchable link-insert helper next to each prose field so the DM finds the right entity and gets the correct id inserted without needing to memorize or hand-type ids. Recommended over auto-detecting known entity names in the text: this exact dataset has a real, elevated false-positive risk for auto-linking (Gerald, Grey, Vel, Fess, Mira, and others are all short, common-word-adjacent real names that would readily false-match inside ordinary prose), and every other inline-linking precedent already in this app (`quest.related[]`, made clickable 2 sessions ago) is explicit DM-authored collection+id pairs, never inferred from text — matching that established convention rather than introducing a new, riskier mechanism. Waiting on confirmation before building this piece specifically, per instruction.
No schema or UI code written this session — waiting on confirmation of 1c (storage shape) and Step 2 (link mechanism) before building, per explicit instruction.

**2026-08-13 (same session as the Combat Tracker investigation below — DM confirmed Tier/Category/Region dropdowns + widened text search, explicitly skipped the CR sort — built and shipped) — `dm.html`.**
DM's answer: "Dropdowns only, skip the sort" — the full recommended set minus the Name/CR sort toggle (CR is only populated on 4/52 creatures, correctly judged not worth a UI control this pass).
**Filter bar.** New `.scc-combat-filters-bar` (3 `<select class="cm-select">`s — Tier/Category/Region — plus a Reset link) inserted above the existing `#scc-pick-combatant` search box, below the "From Session Roster" quick-add chips. Options are populated live by `sccCombatPopulateFilterOptions()`, called from `sccSwitchPanel('combat')` alongside the existing `sccRenderCombatSessionCreatures()` call — same "never hardcode, read from the live in-memory collection every time the panel opens" convention the global search filter bar established 2 sessions ago, so this can't go stale the way Content Manager's own dropdowns did. Tier/Category are scalar columns, deduped and sorted directly. Region required different handling — `creatures.regions` is an array column storing region *ids* (`caparia`/`jugabi`/`nombi`/`sohot`), so the option list is built by flattening+deduping across all 52 creatures' arrays, then each id is resolved to its real display name via the already-loaded `REGIONS` global (id→name lookup) — the dropdown shows "Caparia," not "caparia."
**Search.** Built a dedicated `sccCombatantSearch(query)` rather than extending the shared `sccPickerSearch()` — that function also serves `location`/`npc`/`quest`/`item`, none of which needed or asked for this change, and giving Combat its own function keeps those 4 untouched. Two real behavior changes from the old name-only match: (1) the text query now also checks `habitat`/`description`/`tags` (creatures only, joined into one lowercase haystack) — per the investigation's own finding that `habitat` is real per-creature lore prose, not filter-worthy as a dropdown but genuinely useful as search material; (2) the 3 filters apply to creatures only — a PC object has no `tier`/`category`/`regions` fields, so it's excluded from results whenever any filter is active (implemented as a simple `filtersActive` flag gating the PC branch, not per-field special-casing, since a PC could never satisfy a `c.tier === selectedValue` check anyway). Also picked up the same "browse-by-filter with an empty query" behavior the global search got 2 sessions ago (a direct, low-risk consequence of the filters existing, not separately requested) — setting Tier alone with nothing typed now opens the dropdown and lists every match.
**Audit mode.** Added `.scc-audit-mode .scc-combat-filters-bar { display:none !important; }` alongside the pre-existing `.scc-audit-mode .scc-picker-search` rule, so the new filter bar disappears the same way the search box it sits above already does when viewing a locked historical session — an orphaned filter bar above a hidden search box would have looked broken otherwise.
**Verification, all live.** Local server (fresh port, confirmed via `curl` before trusting it, per the standing lesson). All 3 dropdowns populated with the real values confirmed in the investigation (11 tiers, 12 categories, 4 regions with real names). Set Tier=humanoid with no typed query — correctly returned exactly the 2 real humanoid creatures (Grusk Enforcer, Grusk Color Extractor) and zero PCs, confirming both the browse-by-filter behavior and the PC-exclusion-when-filtered behavior in one check. Set Region=jugabi alone — correctly returned real jugabi-tagged creatures. Reset, then searched "rainforest" with no filters — correctly surfaced 3 real creatures (Jugabi Canopy Runners, Canopy Dancers, Dodooti Hornbeetles) purely via their habitat text, none of which contain "rainforest" in their name, confirming the widened match is doing real work and not coincidentally matching on name. Clicked a filtered result (Mirrenflow Puffalumps, Category=aquatic) through the real dropdown UI — landed correctly in the real encounter roster via the existing, untouched `sccPickerSelect`/`sccAddToCombatStaging` path, difficulty badge updated, then removed via the real remove button. Confirmed audit-mode hides the new bar via computed style (`display:none`). Zero console errors throughout. Zero Supabase writes needed cleanup — combat staging is pure client-side state until "Start Combat," unchanged by this pass, confirmed nothing was left in `SCC_COMBATANTS` afterward. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 extracted inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-13 (new session, Combat Tracker filters — investigation only, no code changed, waiting on go-ahead for the recommended filter set) — `dm.html`, live Supabase queries against `creatures`/`nations`/`world_characters`.**
Explicit investigate-then-report instruction, all 4 sub-questions checked against real data, nothing built.
**a) CR — still 4/52, confirmed via a fresh count, not recalled.** Queried which 4: Grey Fluffets, Still Frogs (both CR 0, tier `dimmed`), Grusk Enforcer (CR 0.5), Grusk Color Extractor (CR 1) — both humanoid/hostile. Cross-checked `ac`/`hp_max`/`speed_ft`/`creature_type`/`size`/`creature_alignment`/`xp_value` and every one of them is *also* exactly 4/52, the same 4 rows — confirms this isn't CR specifically being under-filled, it's that only 4 of 52 creatures have any real D&D stat block at all; the other 48 are lore-only entries (name/tier/category/regions/habitat/description/tags, no mechanics). Given that, a CR **filter** would only ever bucket the same 4 creatures into 3 dropdown values (0, 0.5, 1) — zero real utility, and an empty-feeling dropdown for the other 48. Recommended a Name/CR **sort toggle** instead (creatures with no CR sort last, alphabetical) — surfaces "this one's combat-ready" without hiding anything or presenting a near-empty filter.
**b) Real populated columns beyond CR.** Ran a single population-count query across every plausibly-useful column: `tier` 52/52, `category` 52/52, `regions[]` 52/52 (non-empty), `habitat` 52/52, `tags[]` 52/52 — all fully populated, a materially better picture than "only category and tier are usable." `tier` resolves to 11 real values with a real distribution (common 21, ancient 7, uncommon/corrupted/merry 4 each, dimmed/rare 3 each, sparked/humanoid 2 each, unseen/unknown 1 each) — a genuinely usable dropdown. `category` resolves to 12 real values, similarly distributed (region-exclusive 9, sky/aquatic/domestic 6 each, ancient-mythic 5, the-merry/vareth-corrupted/underground 4 each, the-dimmed 3, hostile/the-sparked 2 each, the-unseen 1) — also genuinely usable. Neither is currently wired into the Combat tab's own add-to-encounter search (`sccPickerSearch('combatant', ...)`, `dm.html:12039`+`12029`) — that box is pure name-substring matching against creatures+PCs today, zero filters of any kind. (Both *are* already exposed as filters on the unrelated global header search, built 2 sessions ago — confirmed this pass would be additive to a different UI surface, not a duplicate build.) `habitat` and `tags[]`, despite being 52/52 populated, are **not** dropdown-worthy: `habitat` is unique free-text lore prose per creature (52 distinct values, confirmed via a full `group by`, e.g. "Bumti Bay, south of Sohot — open water, warm currents") — a dropdown would just be 52 single-item options. `tags[]` overlaps heavily with tier/category plus a long tail of one-off flavor tags (`omen`, `prophetic`, `wanderkeep-indicator`, `navigation`) — same problem. Both are real, good material for widening the search box's own text match (currently `item.name` only) rather than for a new dropdown — a DM typing "rainforest" or "vareth" today finds nothing; matching against habitat/description/tags too would fix that for free, no new UI.
**c) `regions[]` as the nation analog — confirmed usable, not just theoretically closest.** `regions[]` (array column) is 52/52 populated and resolves to exactly the 4 real region ids already known from elsewhere in the app — caparia (41 creatures), nombi (30), sohot (29), jugabi (26) — summing well past 52 confirms most creatures carry 2–3 regions each, not a 1:1 mapping. Checked whether nation-level tagging exists anywhere on creatures instead (it doesn't — no column), and confirmed via the real `nations` table that nations are the *finer* level, each pinned to exactly one of these 4 regions (`nations.region`, 15 real nations, e.g. Solenmere/Bunari/Zippan/Dingurei/Janiveth/Opuri all → caparia) — so "regions[]" isn't a rough stand-in for "nation," it's a real, coarser, already-correct level for "find creatures near here" given creatures were never tagged at the nation level to begin with. Recommended as a third real dropdown filter.
**d) NPCs-in-combat — not investigated, per instruction.** Confirmed (not re-derived from the ask's own framing, checked directly) that `world_characters` has zero columns matching `hp_max`/`ac`/`challenge_rating`/`cr`/`hp`/`armor_class` — the premise going in was accurate. Left untouched, flagged as needing new schema before it's buildable, same framing as before.
**Recommended filter/search set sent for go-ahead, nothing built yet**: Tier dropdown (11 real values), Category dropdown (12 real values), Region dropdown (4 real values — the confirmed nation substitute), a Name/CR sort toggle in place of a CR filter, and widening the existing search box's text match to also check `habitat`/`description`/`tags` (not just `name`). Explicitly not recommending: a nation filter (no schema — would need creatures re-tagged at nation granularity), an army filter (doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase, confirmed), race/level filters (level is a PC-only concept, doesn't apply to creatures; "race" has no clean creature column — `creature_type` is the closest and is only 4/52 populated, same sparsity problem as CR), or any NPC-in-combat work (explicitly out of scope this pass, needs new `world_characters` schema first). Waiting on the DM's go-ahead before writing any UI code.

**2026-08-13 (same session as the Vendor/Shop investigation below — DM answered all 4 design questions, built and shipped) — `dm.html`, new `vendor_stock` table.**
DM's 4 answers: (1) fixed per-vendor stock list with quantity/price overrides — Recommended option, confirmed. (2) no new vendor flag on `pois`/`organizations` — Recommended option, confirmed; vendor-hood is implied purely by having `vendor_stock` rows. (3) buy only, no sell-back — the more conservative of the two options, confirmed explicitly rather than the recommended default. (4) woven into the existing lookup drawer rather than a new top-level tab — Recommended option, confirmed.
**Schema.** `vendor_stock` (`id uuid pk`, `entity_collection text` — `'pois'` or `'organizations'`, `entity_id text`, `item_id text references items(id)`, `price_gp_override numeric null` — null means "use `items.cost_gp`", `quantity integer null` — null means unlimited, `notes text null`, `sort_order`, `created_at`/`updated_at`). RLS mirrors `session_note_entries`' anon-all-access policies exactly (confirmed via `pg_policies` before writing, not assumed). `updated_at` trigger reuses `session_note_entries`' own `update_session_notes_timestamp()` function rather than defining a duplicate — the first migration attempt tried `moddatetime()` (a common Postgres extension trigger function) and failed with "function does not exist," since this project doesn't have that extension enabled; checked what `session_note_entries` actually uses instead of guessing again, per the 2-attempt rule.
**Fetch.** `sccInit()`'s existing `Promise.all` gained `sccFetch('vendor_stock', '&order=sort_order.asc')` into a new `window.VENDOR_STOCK` global — same shape as the `species`/`items`/`conditions` fetches already there.
**UI.** New `sccVendorShopSectionHtml(collection, entityId)` builds a "Shop" `.scc-ql-section` — a shared buyer `<select>` sourced from the real `SCC_PLAYERS` roster (hidden if no players are loaded), one row per stocked item (thumbnail or fallback initial, name + catalog price shown alongside two small editable inputs — Price(gp) and Qty, both blank meaning "use catalog default" / "unlimited," blur-to-save via a new generic `sccVendorSetField(stockId, field, value)`), and a qty-to-buy input + Buy button that's replaced with an "Out of stock" / "No price set" / "No players" label whenever a purchase genuinely isn't possible right now. The add-to-stock search box reuses `sccLootItemSearch()`'s exact `.scc-picker-search`/`.scc-picker-dropdown`/`.scc-picker-option` markup and CSS verbatim — zero new dropdown styling needed, only a new `sccVendorSearchStock()`/`sccVendorAddStock()` pair filtering out items already in that entity's stock. Wired into `sccOpenLookup()`'s `city` branch (POI-scoped, see below) and `organization` branch (unconditional), both appended right after their existing Tags section.
**POI-scoping bug, caught before shipping.** The `city` branch's own `loc` lookup is built fresh inline (`CITIES.concat(POIS).concat(NATIONS)`) and never receives the `_srcType` tag a *different*, unrelated function attaches to its own separately-built array elsewhere in the file — confirmed via grep before trusting it, since the branch's existing (and, this reveals, already-quietly-broken) POI-vs-city type-label logic depends on exactly that field being present. Rather than reuse a check that was never actually working, scoped the Shop section with a direct, reliable membership test instead: `POIS.some(function(p){ return p.id === loc.id; })`. Did not fix the branch's pre-existing broken type-label fallback itself — unrelated to this ask, flagged here rather than touched silently.
**Transaction.** `sccVendorBuy()` composes the same two calls `sccAwardCoins()`/`sccAwardItem()` already make elsewhere in this file — `sccPatch('character_sheet', ...)` for gold, `sccPost('inventory', ...)` for the grant (`dm_granted:false`, since this is a real purchase, not a DM freebie) — with two guards neither of those functions needed: an affordability check against the buyer's real `gold_cp` (blocks with a toast, zero writes, if short) and a stock-quantity check (blocks if the requested buy-quantity exceeds what's left). `vendor_stock.quantity` is decremented after a successful purchase only when it isn't null. No sell-back path exists anywhere in the new code, per the confirmed buy-only scope. Every mutating action (add stock, edit a field, remove stock, buy) ends by re-calling `sccOpenLookup()` on the same entity so the drawer reflects the fresh state immediately, matching this file's existing re-render-in-place convention (tag notes, scenario editor).
**Verification, all live, not assumed.** Local server: a stale server was already running on the usual dev port from an earlier session (the same "may be serving a different checkout" risk flagged before) — started a fresh one on a new port and confirmed via `curl | grep -c` before trusting it. Opened Mira Pont's Sundry Goods (the same POI whose informal `"shop"` tag drove the original investigation) through the real UI, searched and added a real Torch to its stock, confirmed via SQL it landed with `price_gp_override:null, quantity:null`. Bought 1 as Hana Sable through the real Buy button — confirmed via SQL her `gold_cp` dropped 900→899 cp (exactly Torch's real 0.01gp price) and a real `inventory` row was inserted (`dm_granted:false`). Set an unaffordable 10000gp price override and clicked Buy again — confirmed via SQL zero writes occurred on either table, toast correctly blocked it. Set quantity to 0 — confirmed the row correctly flipped to "Out of stock" with Buy hidden. Opened a real Organization (Goldenway) and confirmed its own Shop section renders correctly with `entity_collection:'organizations'`. Opened a real City (Gobblewump Crossing — confirmed via `POIS.some()` it is genuinely not a POI) and confirmed the Shop section correctly does not render, validating the POI-scoping fix. Zero console errors throughout. All test data (the stock row, Hana's inventory row, her gold) reverted via SQL, confirmed clean via a final read. Pre-push checks clean: raw parse, `new Function()` on all 16 extracted inline script blocks, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep empty.

**2026-08-12 (new session, Vendor/Shop investigation — no code changed, 4 real design questions sent to the DM before building anything) — `dm.html`, `sheet/index.html`, live Supabase queries.**
Explicit investigate-then-report instruction, no schema/UI written this session.
**1a — Vendor flag/field check.** Confirmed zero: no vendor/shop column on `pois` or `organizations`. Real `poi_type` values across all 13 real POIs: `town`/`dark-zone`/`site`/`landmark`/`waystation` — no "shop"/"vendor" enum value exists or has ever been used. `organizations.org_type` is 100% null (already known from an earlier session). The one real shop, Mira Pont's Sundry Goods, is `poi_type:"site"` with shop-ness conveyed only in free-text `summary` ("General trade stall that became a semi-permanent shop..."). Real, useful signal found: her POI row already carries a bare `"shop"` string in its `tags` array — an existing, if informal, DM convention, not a structured flag. Her actual stock (12 items + 4 drinks, with real per-item notes like "4 in stock", "1 available, slightly used") lives only as a hardcoded HTML table in the Field Guide document (`dm.html:3541-3557`) — not one row is linked to the real `items` catalog table.
**1b — Gold handling.** `character_sheet.gold_cp` (integer, copper-piece unit — `gp*100 + sp*10 + cp`) is the single real column. `sheet/index.html`'s `renderGold()`/`onCurrencyBlur()` (3-pill Figma UI) reads/writes it via the generic `saveSheet({gold_cp: ...})` PATCH helper. Separately, and more directly relevant: `dm.html`'s `sccAwardCoins()` (existing DM-side loot-award flow) *already* does the exact arithmetic a purchase needs — reads `SCC_SHEETS[player.id].gold_cp`, computes a new total, `sccPatch('character_sheet', ...)`. A "spend gold" action is this same call with subtraction instead of addition, plus a sufficient-funds check — not new plumbing.
**1c — `items.cost_gp` real population.** 127/139 non-null (12 null — mostly non-physical "DM Reference" misc rows and a few intentionally-unpriced flavor items like Ashbloom/Goldbell). Of the 127, 18 are exactly `0.00` — almost all `category:"accessory"` cosmetic-slot rows from the Aug 10 appearance-catalog migration (Monk's Gi, Robes, Simple Tunic, etc.), not real shop-worthy prices; a vendor build should treat `cost_gp` null-or-zero as "not for sale by default," not "free." 12 rows carry real fractional gp values (`0.01`/`0.05`/`0.1`/`0.2`/`0.5`) — checked these are genuine PHB-accurate prices (Torch 1cp, Dart 5cp, Rations 5sp, Javelin 5sp), not a data error, and convert cleanly to whole copper (`cost_gp * 100` is always a clean integer in the real data) so no floating-point concern doing the math against `gold_cp`.
**1d — Reusable UI pattern.** `sheet/index.html`'s +Add Item modal (`runInventorySearch()`/`selectCatalogItem()`) is the closest real precedent for browse-and-buy: name+category search scoped to `player_facing:true` items, thumbnail/name/category/cost/weight per row, qty input, one-click add that POSTs a real `inventory` row. `dm.html`'s `sccAwardItem()` is the DM-side equivalent (POSTs `{character_id, token, item_id, quantity, dm_granted:true}`) — confirms the exact insert shape a purchase would reuse, just without `dm_granted` and with a preceding gold check. Neither `sccAwardCoins()` nor `sccAwardItem()` is wrapped in a real DB transaction — two independent REST calls each — a pre-existing pattern in this codebase, not a new risk a vendor system would introduce, but flagged since a purchase (deduct gold + grant item) has the same non-atomicity and it's worth the DM knowing that going in.
**Step 2 — 4 questions sent, not guessed at:**
1. Stock model — fixed per-vendor item list (with optional price/quantity override) vs. rule-based ("everything in category X"). Recommended fixed list: the one real example (Mira Pont) is hand-curated with per-item notes and partial stock counts, which a category-rule can't express, and a rule risks surfacing items that make no narrative sense for a given vendor.
2. Whether vendor-hood needs a new boolean flag on `pois`/`organizations`, or whether simply having rows in a new stock table is sufficient signal on its own (no flag to drift out of sync with actual data). Recommended the latter.
3. Buy-only (as literally described in the ask) vs. also building sell-back. Not recommended either way — flagged that Mira Pont's own signage ("FAIR PRICES — NO HAGGLING") is a real characterization detail that could inform this later, but that's not a substitute for the DM's actual answer.
4. UI placement — new top-level nav tab (matching Setup's precedent from the same night) vs. woven into the existing lookup drawer for vendor-flagged entities. Recommended the lookup drawer: unlike Setup (used every session), vendor scenes are rare/special-cased, and the drawer is already where a DM's attention lands when they look up a specific shop.
No schema or UI written this session — waiting on the DM's answers to the above before proceeding, per explicit instruction.

**2026-08-12 (new session, Session Setup — nav restructure + 2 related gaps found in the investigation) — `dm.html`.**
4 pieces, investigate-first on the nav structure per explicit instruction.
**1 — Investigation.** Read the real structure before changing anything: Setup was `#scc-panel-setup`, one of 8 `.scc-tab-btn` sub-panels (Setup/NPCs/Locations/Quests/Combat/Notes/Live Session/Field Guide) switched via `sccSwitchPanel()`, all nested inside the single top-level `#form-session` `.dm-form` and sharing its `.scc-header` (title, session-history dropdown, the 8-button sub-nav itself) and always-visible `.scc-player-bar`. Confirmed via grep that nothing outside `dm.html:2769-2881` referenced `scc-panel-setup` by id/class, and that `SCC_SELECTED`/`sccPickerSelect()`/`sccRemoveChip()` are plain JS globals with no DOM-nesting assumptions — meaning the panel's picker state would keep working correctly regardless of which top-level tab physically contained its markup, confirmed before, not after, moving anything.
**2 — Nav restructure.** New top-level tab `data-tab="setup"` → `#form-setup`, positioned between Players and Session in both the button row and the DOM. Moved `#scc-panel-setup`'s entire contents verbatim (session number/date, prep notes, all 5 entity pickers, save button, session-complete bar) — same ids, same classes, same `scc-panel-inner` wrapper for consistent padding, just the outer `.scc-panel`/`sccSwitchPanel`-toggle wrapper dropped since it's a real top-level `.dm-form` now, toggled by the existing generic `switchTab()` exactly like Manage/Quests/Players. Removed the "Setup" button from the 8-button sub-nav; NPCs is now the sub-nav's default-active panel. `switchTab`'s `sccInit()` trigger (previously `tab === 'session'` only) now also fires on `tab === 'setup'`, since Setup needs the same live `CHARACTERS`/`CITIES`/etc. data and `SCC_SESSION` state the Session tab already loads.
**Dependency found and fixed, not asked for but required by the move**: `.scc-audit-mode` (the read-only lockdown applied while viewing a past/completed session via Session History) was only ever added to `#form-session` in `sccLoadHistorySession()`/removed in `sccLoadCurrentSession()`. Since Setup is now a separate top-level form, leaving this unfixed would have meant a completed/audited session's setup silently stayed live-editable on the new page — the opposite of what audit mode is for. Now toggled on `#form-setup` too, in both functions. Also added one narrow new audit-mode rule (`.scc-audit-mode .scc-scenario-editor-group .scc-tagnote-del { display:none }`) since the new scenario editor's delete buttons aren't a class already covered by the existing blanket `.scc-input`/`.scc-textarea`/`.scc-browse-btn`/`.scc-setup-actions` audit-mode hides — without it, a DM viewing a locked session could still delete its scenario data.
**3 — `session_scenarios` editor.** Investigated the real shape first (queried live data, not assumed): `[{group: string, scenarios: [{action: string, response: string}]}]` — 8 groups, 44 action/response rows on the one real session. Matches exactly what `sccLiveRenderScenarios()` (Live Session panel) already reads, and what the code's own comment already admitted was missing: *"No scenarios loaded for this session. Add them in Supabase session_notes.session_scenarios."* New editor inside the Setup page — collapsible group cards (matching the read-side's own collapse pattern), each with an editable group-name input and its action/response rows, `+ Add Group`/`+ Add Scenario`/delete on both levels. Edits go into a local working copy (`SCC_SCENARIO_EDIT`, deep-cloned on load) and save as one PATCH via `sccSaveScenarios()` — not autosaved per keystroke, since a single group can hold 9+ rows and a field-by-field save would be both wasteful and janky mid-edit.
**4 — Combat roster wiring.** Confirmed the gap precisely first: `sccPickerSearch('combatant', query)` requires a typed query and has zero awareness of `SCC_SELECTED.creature` (Setup's assigned-creatures list) at all. Added `sccRenderCombatSessionCreatures()` — renders Setup's assigned creatures as one-click "+ Name" quick-add chips above the existing search box, reusing the exact same `sccAddToCombatStaging()` the search dropdown already calls (not a parallel add path); a creature already in the encounter shows as a disabled "✓ Name" instead of vanishing, so the roster stays visible as a reference. Hooked into `sccSwitchPanel('combat')` (fresh render on panel open) and into `sccRenderStagingList()` itself (so removing a combatant re-enables its quick-add chip immediately). Full search stays untouched below it for anything not pre-assigned, or for players.
**Verification, all live**: Setup confirmed reachable from its own nav button, real session data (session 1, all 5 pickers' real chips) populated correctly on load — screenshotted. Scenario editor: expanded a real group (5 real rows), edited one response with a test marker, saved, confirmed the marker landed in Supabase via direct SQL, then confirmed the Live Session panel's Scenarios search picked it up via a **fresh Supabase fetch** (switched tabs, which re-triggers `sccInit()`) rather than trusting client-side state — screenshotted the match. Reverted the marker via a scoped `jsonb_set` (touched only that one field, confirmed the other 7 groups and the touched group's other 4 rows were untouched, and the array's `jsonb_array_length` was still 8 afterward). Combat: confirmed all 6 real Setup-assigned creatures rendered as quick-add chips, clicked "Grey Fluffets" with zero search typed, confirmed it landed as a real combatant row with CR/DEX/Init/Loot controls — screenshotted. Confirmed full-text search still works for a non-assigned creature (Bounce Beetles). Confirmed audit-mode lockdown applies to the new Setup page (`sccLoadHistorySession(1)` → both forms carry `.scc-audit-mode`, date input hides) and correctly releases on `sccLoadCurrentSession()`. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push syntax check clean.

**2026-08-12 (new session, Note-taking — tagging + filterable view, @mention explicitly deferred) — `dm.html`, new `session_note_entries` table.**
Ask was structured explicitly as investigate → report a data-model recommendation → build. Followed that order.
**Investigation (`sheet/index.html`)**: `character_notes` (real schema: `id, character_id, token, tab, title, content, sort_order, is_favorite`) is genuinely one-row-per-note. `tab` is a single-select category (`general/places/names/things/misc`, `NOTE_TABS` array) — not multi-tag — rendered as a tab bar (`renderNotes()`/`buildNoteCardHtml()`), with a *separate* always-on cross-tab free-text search (`onNotesSearchInput()`, searches title+content regardless of active tab) layered on top. Cards support inline title/body editing (autosave on blur/1.2s debounce), pin/favorite, drag-reorder, delete via a shared confirm modal. Checked `session_notes`' real schema next — genuinely different shape, not just superficially: one row per `session_number`, and the 5 note fields (`player_decisions/unexpected_moments/loose_threads/follow_up_needed/milestone_notes`) are only part of it — the same row also carries Session Setup's entire state (`location_ids/npc_ids/creature_ids/quest_ids_active/item_ids_awarded`), `session_status` (drives the audit-lock/read-only view), `session_scenarios` (read by the Field Guide accordion), `premise`/`prep_notes`/`npc_relationships`. Confirmed via a real row count check: only 1 row exists (session 1), all 5 note fields currently null — low data risk, but that's not the real constraint.
**Recommendation (stated, not silently decided)**: do not restructure `session_notes` to one-row-per-note. It isn't a notes table with some extra columns — it's the session's master record, and Setup/Field Guide/session-history/audit-lock are all built directly on its one-row-per-session shape. Reshaping it would ripple through every one of those unrelated features, which is the opposite of "small and independent." The 5 existing fields are also still real and load-bearing today — `sccCopyPostSessionSummary()` reads all 5 to build the DM's copyable end-of-session recap, and `sccLiveSaveNote()` (the Quick Note feature, fixed 4 sessions ago) writes directly into `player_decisions` — collapsing them into freeform tagged notes would break both without being asked to. Instead: added a new table, `session_note_entries` (`id, session_number, category, title, content, created_at, updated_at`, RLS policies mirroring `session_notes`' own anon-all-access set, `updated_at` trigger reusing `session_notes`' existing trigger function rather than writing a duplicate), scoped by `session_number` the same way every other session-scoped panel already works. This cleanly reuses `character_notes`' proven *pattern* (one row per note, single-select category, autosave, cross-item search) without entangling it with `session_notes`' unrelated columns, and leaves the 5 structured fields exactly as they were.
**Categories**: folded the ask's 4 (DM-Only, Player Actions, Quest-Related, Follow-Up) together with the 5 existing textarea themes rather than running two overlapping vocabularies — merged the two genuine near-duplicates (Player Decisions → Player Actions, Follow-Up Needed → Follow-Up) and kept the 3 with no equivalent in the ask's list as their own tags (Unexpected Moment, Loose Thread, Milestone). Net: 7 categories, single-select per note (matching `character_notes`' own `tab` shape, not multi-tag — the ask didn't ask for multi-tag and `character_notes` doesn't do it either). DM-Only badge reuses `var(--cyan)` (established 3 sessions ago as THE app-wide DM-Only color) and Quest-Related reuses `var(--gold)` (established as the quest-related accent when the NPC-panel icon was built) — not new colors, matching what those two labels already mean everywhere else in the app. The other 5 categories share one neutral badge style rather than inventing 5 more arbitrary hues with no established meaning.
**Build**: new "Tagged Notes" block inside the existing Notes panel, below the 5 structured fields (not replacing them) — search box, category dropdown + "+ New Note", filter chips with live counts (`sccRenderTagNoteFilters()`), a card per note (badge, relative timestamp, copy icon, delete icon, inline-editable title + body, autosave on change) via `sccBuildTagNoteCard()`/`sccSaveTagNoteField()`. No pin/favorite/drag-reorder — `character_notes` has them, this ask didn't request them, so they weren't added. Whole section respects the existing `.scc-audit-mode` read-only convention (hidden wholesale while viewing a past/locked session, consistent with how the 5 structured fields already behave there — not a new inconsistency). `sccSwitchPanel('notes')` now calls `sccRenderTaggedNotes()`, matching the existing `'live'`/`'fieldguide'` panel-open-triggers-fetch pattern.
**Verification, all live**: added 4 real notes spanning 4 different categories (one via full real click-and-type through the actual UI, the rest via the same insert call the "+ New Note" button itself makes — not a mock). Category filter ("DM-Only") correctly narrowed 4→1. Free-text search ("Gerald") correctly narrowed 4→2, matching via body content on one of the two, not just title — confirms the search isn't title-only. Delete removed a real row via the actual button click, confirmed 4→3 via direct SQL, not assumed. Copy-to-clipboard: the sandboxed test browser blocks real OS clipboard access (`navigator.clipboard.readText()` → permission denied) — not a code issue, a harness limitation — so verified correctness by intercepting `navigator.clipboard.writeText` directly and confirming the exact expected text (`"[Quest-Related] Greytalon disinformation play\n..."`) was passed, plus the real success toast fired. All 4 test notes deleted afterward via SQL — none of this was real campaign content. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push syntax check clean; caught and fixed one of my own literal-emoji slips in a new `.textContent`-bound string before committing (`✓` escape, not the raw glyph — per this file's own C2 rule).

**2026-08-12 (new session, Quest Manager — 4 independent gap fixes, no new architecture) — `dm.html`, `quests` table (`suggested_level` migration).**
All 4 items from the investigation, closed in one session per the DM's explicit "no new architecture needed" framing — which turned out to require correcting one premise (see item 2) rather than literally following it.
**1 — Clickable related-entity tags.** `renderQuestDetail()`'s `relatedHtml` used to render `quest.related[]` as inert `<span class="quest-related-tag">collection: id</span>` — no click handler, raw id as the label. New `sccRelatedCollectionInfo(collection)` maps every real Supabase collection name (`characters/cities/pois/nations/creatures/organizations/items/quests/regions/religions/species`) to its `sccOpenLookup()` type key and source array — confirmed via `select distinct jsonb_array_elements(related)->>'collection'` that only 4 are in real use today (`organizations/cities/creatures/characters`), but mapped all 11 so a future DM-added link doesn't silently render inert again. Each tag now resolves and shows the real entity name (falls back to the raw id if not found) and calls `sccOpenLookup(type, id)` on click; unrecognized collections (none exist today) render inert with an explanatory tooltip instead of a broken click. Added `:hover` state to `.quest-related-tag` since it's now interactive.
**Hard dependency, fixed as part of this item**: testing the first click surfaced that the lookup drawer still doesn't paint on the Quests tab — the tab-visibility bug flagged 2 sessions ago (`#scc-lookup-panel`/`#scc-lookup-backdrop` nested inside `#form-session`, `display:none` on every other tab) was still unfixed and sat directly on the feature being built (related tags live on the Quest Dashboard, not the Session tab). Moved both elements to page level — direct children of `.dm-main`, before `.dm-forms-panel` — so they're siblings of every tab's form instead of nested in one. Confirmed only one `id="scc-lookup-panel"`/`id="scc-lookup-backdrop"` each post-move (no duplicate-id risk), and re-verified the drawer still renders correctly from the Session tab (its original working context) as well as Quests (previously broken) and Manage (fixed last session for Org/Item, same underlying bug).
**2 — `suggested_level`.** Migration `add_quests_suggested_level`: `quests.suggested_level integer null`, all 10 existing quests unaffected (confirmed null on all after migration). The ask said "add it to the Content Manager's quest edit form" — reading the actual Content Manager code first (per C1) found this premise doesn't hold: quests were never part of the general Add/Edit Entry form at all (that form only covers city/creature/poi/character/organization/item), and the *only* quest-content mutation anywhere in the DM Tools UI is the quest detail view's own inline `status` selector (`setQuestStatus()`). Building a real Content Manager quest form would itself be new architecture, directly contradicting the DM's own stated constraint for this pass — so `suggested_level` was added as a second inline editor next to `status`, same pattern (`setQuestSuggestedLevel()` mirrors `setQuestStatus()`'s fetch/PATCH shape exactly), and shown as a gold `Suggested Level N` badge in the meta row when set. Empty/null shows as a blank `—`-placeholder input, not `0` or any other misleading default.
**3 — Malformed `stakes` data.** Both real offenders confirmed via direct query: `follow-the-dran-lead` had *"Identifying who commissioned the color extraction at Gobblewump Crossing — and what they intended to do with the harvested Featherflow color."* (a hook sentence, not an enum value); `the-letter-for-vel` had *"Unknown — the significance of the letter and what Vel is waiting for is not yet established."* (an honest DM placeholder, not an enum value). Neither was a guess — inferred from real content against the other 8 quests' real stakes values as a calibration set (queried and compared, not assumed): **`follow-the-dran-lead` → `mid`.** It's the direct narrative continuation of "Something Is Wrong With Gerald" (already `mid`) — its own DM notes state *"Dran is not the top of this chain"*, explicitly deferring the real payoff/reveal to a later, not-yet-written quest (Sessions 3–4). That rules out `high` (the one real `high` quest, Anomaly 7-C, is framed as already possessing a complete, ready-to-use piece of major-network intelligence — a payoff, not an open thread) — and its 3-beat/300xp shape is smaller than the standard 4-beat/375xp template, consistent with a continuation step rather than a standalone arc. **`the-letter-for-vel` → `low`.** Its own DM notes call it a *"Future-session plant... do not develop this in Session 1... Vel's full backstory is not yet written"* — 0 XP, 2 skeletal beats, zero established world-level consequence in the text (every real `mid`/`high` quest names a concrete threat or stakes; this one explicitly doesn't yet). As currently written it's a single NPC's personal, contained mystery — matching the same narrow scope as the two other real `low` quests (a merchant's lost shipment, an archivist's private curiosity) far better than any `mid`/`high` quest's town- or faction-level stakes. Applied via direct SQL (no stakes editor exists in any UI, confirmed — this was corrective data cleanup, not something available to fix through the app).
**4 — `player_facing` confirmation.** Re-verified, not recalled from memory: `select player_facing, count(*) from quests group by player_facing` → still 10/10 `false`; `grep quest js/router.js js/wiki.js` → still zero matches in both. Both facts logged explicitly in Current Focus and Next Priorities as a named, unaddressed gap (not built this pass, per the DM's own instruction) rather than left as silent debt.
**Verification, all live**: on "The Greytalon" (6 related entries spanning cities/characters ×3/creatures/organizations — the richest real test case), clicked "Torv Bassle" (character) and "Greytalons" (creature) through the real rendered tags — both opened the correct real card, both visibly painted while still on the Quests tab (proof the drawer fix works, not just the click handler). Set `suggested_level` to 4 via the real input, confirmed the toast, confirmed via direct SQL it landed in Supabase, confirmed via a full page reload that the badge and input both show `4` correctly, then reverted to `null` via SQL (test value, not a real DM decision — unlike the stakes fix, which is real corrective data and was kept). Confirmed the Stakes filter now correctly buckets both corrected quests (`mid` filter includes Follow the Dran Lead, `low` filter includes The Letter for Vel). Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push syntax check clean; one literal-emoji slip caught in the new `setQuestSuggestedLevel()` (mirrored from `setQuestStatus()`'s existing `.textContent` pattern) — fixed to `✓`/`⚠` JS escapes rather than leaving a literal glyph in a new addition, per C2 (pre-existing literal emoji in `setQuestStatus()` itself left untouched, not this session's scope). Committed and pushed to `anavale-wiki-scc-ux`.
