**2026-08-12 (new session, Search+Preview "easy slice" — filters + missing entity coverage) — `dm.html`: (1) added filter dropdowns to the global search — Type selector plus per-type secondary filters, all option lists computed live from the in-memory collections each time the filter bar opens rather than hardcoded. (2) `sccGlobalSearch()` now also searches Regions/Religions/Species. (3) `sccOpenLookup()` gained matching render branches for those 3 types. "Open in new tab" explicitly deferred per the ask — routing/visibility issues from the original investigation (no `#quest` route, most DM content is `player_facing:false`) still need a real decision first.**
Scoped exactly to the "easy slice": filters + missing entity search coverage, not the new-tab piece.
**Filter dropdowns**: added a `⚙` toggle next to the global search box that reveals a filter bar — a Type selector (NPC/Location/Creature/Organization/Item/Quest) plus per-type secondary `<select>`s that show/hide based on the chosen type, mirroring the show/hide pattern Content Manager's own `cmTypeFilterChanged()` already uses. Fields, exactly as specified: NPC (category, player_facing), City (region, nation, city_type, player_facing), Creature (category, tier, player_facing), Organization (alignment — `org_type` skipped, confirmed 100% null), Item (category, rarity, equip_slot), Quest (status). Every option list is built by a `distinct()` scan over the real live `CHARACTERS`/`CITIES`/`CREATURES`/`ORGANIZATIONS`/`ITEMS`/`QUESTS` arrays each time the filter bar opens (`sccGlobalPopulateFilterOptions()`) — never hardcoded — so it can't drift from real data the way Content Manager's own `color_health`/item-category dropdowns already have (confirmed live: the new Item Category list correctly includes `misc`, which Content Manager's dropdown is missing). Quest Status correctly resolves to only `available`/`dormant` since those are the only two real values in the data today — no dead `active`/`completed` options shown. `player_facing` filters are a plain Visible/DM-Only boolean pair (not dynamically populated, since it's a real boolean column, not a string enum — and the `'teaser'` value used elsewhere in this file's badge logic doesn't correspond to any real data, confirmed via the original audit). Filtering itself is applied via `sccGlobalFieldMatcher()`, layered into the existing `addCollection()` text-match pass rather than a second filter stage. `sccGlobalSearch()`'s early-return was loosened so the results panel now also opens on an active filter with no typed query, letting the DM browse-by-filter (e.g. "all DM-Only NPCs") — not explicitly asked for but a direct, low-risk consequence of the filters existing at all.
**Missing entity coverage**: `addCollection()` calls added for `REGIONS`/`RELIGIONS`/`SPECIES`, identical name+summary substring pattern as every other type (per the ask, no per-type special-casing) — `SPECIES` has no `summary` column so it's effectively name-only matching for that one type, which is expected and fine, not a bug. `sccOpenLookup()` gained three new branches (region/religion/species) reusing the existing `scc-ql-*` card pattern verbatim: Region reuses the exact color-health badge logic already duplicated in the character and city branches (a third copy, not refactored — out of scope for this pass); Religion and Species both correctly render as fallback-letter portraits with no `<img>` markup at all, since neither table has an `image` column (confirmed via schema query before writing the branch, not assumed from the character/city/item pattern). One real, necessary side-fix: Religion's `practices` field is genuine multi-paragraph prose in real data (confirmed on The Brightcreed) and was rendering as one run-on block, since `.scc-ql-section-body` (shared by every lookup card's prose sections) had no `white-space` rule — added `white-space:pre-wrap`, a safe universal change since it's a no-op for every existing single-paragraph field already using that class.
**Verification, all live, not assumed**: searched and clicked into all 3 new types through the real search-and-click UI (not direct function calls) — Region/Caparia (health badge, visibility badge, summary, tags all correct), Religion/The Brightcreed (multi-paragraph Practices field confirmed rendering with real line breaks post-fix), Species/Veilstepped (PHB Equivalent, Region, Affinity, Description all correct, no tags section since species has no tags column). Item+Category=weapon filter cross-checked against real `ITEMS` data programmatically (not just a visual glance) — confirmed all 20 capped results are genuinely `category:"weapon"`, and confirmed a known non-weapon item (+1 Chain Mail, armor) is excluded once the filter is applied. NPC+DM-Only filter returned exactly 17 results, an exact match to the real count already established in the original color-collision audit — strong confirmation the `player_facing` boolean comparison is correct, not an off-by-some-string-coercion bug. Reset button confirmed clearing both the Type select and all secondary selects and re-closing the results panel. Confirmed zero regression on a plain unfiltered text search (`"gerald"` still returns the expected 9 mixed-type results). Zero console errors throughout, zero Supabase writes (this is a pure client-side feature — nothing to revert). Pre-push `new Function()` syntax check clean on every extracted inline script block, diff-scoped literal-emoji grep came back empty. Committed and pushed to `anavale-wiki-scc-ux`.

Prior session recolored `.scc-fg-box--yellow` to cyan but left its hardcoded label glyph (`&#x1F7E1;`, 🟡 yellow circle) untouched, flagging it as a follow-up since there's no cyan circle emoji in Unicode and swapping ~20 lines of hardcoded document text was bigger than that session's scope. DM asked for it now, with the replacement character specified: `&#x1F4A0;` (💠 diamond shape with a dot inside).
Confirmed via `grep`/`awk` before touching anything that all 18 real occurrences of `&#x1F7E1;` in the file sit immediately inside a `.scc-fg-box--yellow` div with zero exceptions and zero unrelated uses elsewhere — including one non-obvious case, `~dm.html:3569` ("THE LONG GAME"), which uses the same box class but a custom heading instead of literal "DM ONLY" text; included it since it's the same visual/semantic box, just worded differently. Straight `replace_all` on the entity string, no per-line edits needed. `~20` in the ask turned out to be 18 exactly.
**Verification**: `git stash`/`git stash pop` for a true before/after — before showed 🟡 next to "DM ONLY" inside the now-cyan box (confirming the described inconsistency was real and still present), after showed 💠 in the same spot. Read the live DOM text directly (`textContent` + `codePointAt(0)`) and confirmed it resolves to `1f4a0` exactly — not just a visual glance, since a mis-encoded entity can render a similar-looking fallback glyph without being the intended character. Pre-push checks: `new Function()` syntax check clean on every extracted script block, and a diff-scoped grep for literal (non-entity) emoji in the added lines came back empty — the swap only ever touched the existing HTML-entity form, consistent with this file's C2 rule. Zero console errors. Committed and pushed to `anavale-wiki-scc-ux`.

**2026-08-12 (new session, implementing the color resolution from the prior session's report) — DM-Only unified to one new cyan token, a gold quest-related icon added, NPC Hostile/Neutral confirmed to need no new color. `css/tokens.css`, `dm.html`.**
DM made 4 decisions off the prior session's full color-inventory report and asked for implementation: (1) DM-Only gets one new color (`#22d3ee`, picked from that report as the one hue family — cyan — genuinely unused anywhere in the app), replacing its two existing conflicting colors. (2) Quest-related becomes a small gold icon, not a fill/background color, so it doesn't compete with the relationship-color axis. (3) NPC Hostile/Neutral confirmed to just reuse `SCC_REL_CYCLE` as-is, no new token — document this for later, don't build it now. (4) Read Aloud's green stays, per the recommendation given (real semantic fit; both green usages carry their own text label, so the collision risk is low in practice, unlike DM-Only's which was a real same-concept-two-colors inconsistency).
**New token** (`css/tokens.css`, after the `--purple` block): `--cyan: #22d3ee`, `--cyan-dim: rgba(34,211,238,0.15)`, `--cyan-border: rgba(34,211,238,0.35)` — same 3-line shape as the existing `--blue`/`--red` blocks.
**DM-Only recolor** (`dm.html`): `.scc-fg-box--yellow` (Field Guide, `~dm.html:6417`) — background/border/label/body all moved from its old amber values to the new cyan token/rgba equivalents (`rgba(34,211,238,0.07)` bg, `var(--cyan-border)` border, `var(--cyan)` label, `#cffafe` body text — Tailwind cyan-100, matching the existing pattern where every other box's body text is a pastel-100 tint of its own label color). Class name kept as `--yellow` (not renamed) — it's referenced ~20 times in the hardcoded Field Guide document markup, and renaming would mean touching all of them for a purely cosmetic identifier change with no functional benefit; left a comment on the rule explaining the name/color mismatch instead. The "HOW TO USE THIS GUIDE" legend line updated from "Yellow boxes = DM only" to "Cyan boxes = DM only" so the document stays internally accurate. `player_facing` badge (`sccOpenLookup`'s `character` branch, `pfColor`, and `organization` branch, `opfColor`) — both had their DM-Only fallback changed from `var(--red)` to `var(--cyan)`; the Visible (green) and Teaser (gold) states are untouched, only the third state's color moved.
**Flagged, not fixed**: the DM-Only box labels in the Field Guide document still carry a literal `&#x1F7E1;` (🟡 yellow circle) emoji glyph prefix, hardcoded ~20 times — the box's own background/border/label color are now genuinely cyan, but that inline circle glyph is still visually yellow, since there's no cyan circle emoji in Unicode to swap it for. Fixing this means either dropping the circle prefix (matching how blue/purple/orange boxes already work with no emoji prefix) or picking new iconography — a bigger, more visible edit than what was asked (which was scoped to the box's own colors and the badge), so left as a flagged follow-up rather than decided unilaterally. Logged in Next Priorities.
**Quest-related icon** (`dm.html`, `sccRenderNpcPanel`'s card-row template, `~dm.html:11822` + CSS `~dm.html:5453`): new `.scc-npc2-badge-row` wraps the existing relationship badge plus a new conditional `.scc-npc2-quest-icon` span (📜 scroll, `&#128220;` entity per this file's own C2 no-literal-emoji rule — confirmed via grep that *pre-existing* code in this same function already has several literal emoji characters directly in JS strings (🎭📖📜⚠🔒), which appears to be a long-standing, unenforced violation of C2 predating this session; not fixed here since it wasn't asked and touching it is a separate, larger cleanup — but new code in this session follows C2 correctly regardless of what's already there). The icon renders only when `linkedQuests.length` (already computed earlier in the same function from `quests.related`, previously used only inside the expanded DM-only body — this reuses that same data, no new query) is non-zero, colored `var(--gold)`, no background/fill, with a `title` tooltip naming the linked quest(s) — relationship badge's own color is completely untouched. `margin-bottom` moved from `.scc-npc2-rel-badge` onto the new wrapper row so spacing is unchanged when the quest icon isn't present.
**NPC Hostile/Neutral**: no code change — confirmed and documented (Current Focus, Next Priorities) that any future build of this surface reuses `SCC_REL_CYCLE`'s existing 5 colors (`REL_BG` in `dm.html`) rather than inventing a new token, so a later phase doesn't reinvent this.
**Verification**: real before/after via `git stash`/`git stash pop` (not assumed) on all three DM-Only surfaces — Field Guide's Step-1 box, character lookup card (Dran), organization lookup card (The Gigglegloom Conclave) — screenshotted both states for each. Cyan values confirmed via `getComputedStyle()` (`rgb(34, 211, 238)` exactly), not just visual read. Quest icon confirmed live on Gobblewump Gerald's NPC panel card (real quest-linked NPC in the current session roster, found by cross-referencing `SCC_SELECTED.npc` against `quests[].related`) — icon present with the correct quest name in its tooltip, `getComputedStyle()` confirmed `rgb(240, 180, 41)` (`var(--gold)` exactly) on the icon; absent (correctly) on non-quest-linked NPCs (Dran, Fess) in the same screenshot. Pre-push `new Function()` syntax check clean on every extracted inline script block, zero console errors throughout. Committed and pushed to `anavale-wiki-scc-ux`.

**2026-08-12 (new session, immediately after the UX/IA investigation below) — Fixed the 2 real bugs that investigation named, plus a 3rd (`sccSaveNotes()`'s own 409) it exposed mid-fix. `dm.html`.**
DM asked for exactly two fixes named in the prior session's investigation, tested live, logged, committed, pushed.
**Bug 1 — `sccLiveSaveNote()` never persisted (`dm.html:10757`).** The ask was to reuse the existing "Save Notes" write path (`sccSaveNotes()`, writes `session_notes`) rather than invent a new one. Per D2, read that path before reusing it — and live-tested it directly against Supabase (`curl` against `/rest/v1/session_notes` with the exact headers `sccPost()` sends) before wiring anything to it. **Found it was itself broken**: `sccPost('session_notes', body)` sends `Prefer: resolution=merge-duplicates` with no `on_conflict` query param; `session_notes` has `UNIQUE(session_number)` but its primary key is `id` (not in the body), so PostgREST's upsert targets the PK, gets a fresh row every time, and the real conflict (on `session_number`) surfaces as a 409 `23505 duplicate key value violates unique constraint "session_notes_session_number_key"` — confirmed via a live probe POST against the real session-1 row (`player_decisions` temporarily set to a marker string, confirmed via SQL, then reverted to `null`). Since `session_number: 1` already exists (created at Session 0), this means **`sccSaveNotes()`'s "Save Notes" button has been silently 409-failing (toast: "Save failed") for any DM who has ever clicked it against an existing session** — a bug independent of anything asked this session, discovered only because reusing it faithfully would have shipped the same failure into the new Quick Note path. Root-caused, not patched: `sccSaveSetup()` (`dm.html:11636`) already has the correct pattern two functions away — PATCH via `?session_number=eq.N` when `SCC_SESSION.session_number === num`, POST only for a genuinely new session — so `sccSaveNotes()` was given the identical branch (confirmed the fix via a second live probe: same POST body against `?on_conflict=session_number` returns 200 and merges correctly, preserving unrelated columns like `session_status`/`session_date` — confirmed via SQL diff before/after).
`sccLiveSaveNote()` itself: now builds the same timestamped-append string as before, writes it into the DOM textarea (`#scc-note-decisions`) exactly as before so anything already reading that element keeps working, but also `sccPatch('session_notes', '?session_number=eq.'+num, {session_number, player_decisions})` immediately, keyed off `SCC_SESSION.session_number` (always populated by the time the Live Session panel is reachable — no POST branch needed here, unlike `sccSaveNotes()`, since a session must already exist to be live). Bails with a clear "no active session to sync to" toast if `SCC_SESSION` is somehow unset, rather than silently losing the note.
**Bug 2 — `sccOpenLookup()` missing Organization/Item branches (`dm.html:12888`).** Fixed exactly as scoped: `sccGlobalResultClick()`'s bad `organization → 'city'` remap removed (organization now passes through to its own branch; `nation → 'city'` remap kept, since nations genuinely are in the city lookup's combined array). Added `organization` and `item` branches matching the existing character/city/creature/quest pattern exactly (same `scc-ql-header`/`scc-ql-fields`/`scc-ql-section`/`scc-dm-block` classes, no new CSS) — Organization shows the existing `player_facing` DM-Only/Teaser/Visible badge plus alignment/HQ/org_type/leadership fields and summary/purpose/description/history/known_members/gigglegloom_relationship/public_perception prose, secret + dm_notes in the existing DM-only block style, tags. Item shows read_aloud (existing `.scc-ra-block`, same as creatures), category/rarity/cost_gp/weight_lb/equip_slot/damage/base_ac/magic_bonus fields, summary/description/history/gigglegloom_relationship/story_note prose, dm_notes, tags — `items.image` is already a full storage URL (unlike characters/cities, which store just a flag and construct the path from `id`), used directly. Schema for both branches confirmed via live Supabase queries before writing any HTML, not guessed from the investigation's field list alone.
**Bug 3, found but NOT fixed — out of the requested scope, flagged for a DM decision.** While testing bug 2 in the browser, the drawer opened (confirmed via `classList`) but never visually painted. Root cause: `#scc-lookup-panel` is nested inside `div.dm-form.scc-form` — the Session tab's own top-level form container, `display:none` whenever the DM is on Manage/Quests/Players. `sccOpenLookup()`/`sccGlobalSearch()` are themselves tab-agnostic (header search bar renders on every tab, confirmed in the investigation), but the **lookup drawer's markup only ever paints if the DM happens to already be on the Session tab** — same silent-fixed-position-breakage class of bug this file's own Modals rule (§8) already warns about for `sheet/index.html`, just via `display:none` on an ancestor rather than a transform/animation. Not fixed — outside the two named bugs — but logged here and in Next Priorities since it affects every entity type's lookup, not just the two touched this session.
**Verification, all live, not assumed**: local static server was found to be serving the wrong checkout (`anavale-wiki`, not this session's actual `anavale-wiki-scc-ux` working directory — a pre-existing environment mismatch, not a code issue) — caught via `sccOpenLookup.toString()` not containing the new branches despite `grep` confirming they were on disk; worked around by starting a second local server rooted at the correct directory rather than touching the other one (may be serving another session). With that resolved: Organization (Goldenway) and Item (Health Potion) both confirmed rendering full real content via the actual search-and-click UI, screenshotted. Quick Note: typed a marker note on the Live Session tab, saved, confirmed via direct SQL the marker landed in `session_notes.player_decisions` immediately (before ever touching the Notes tab), then did a full page reload and confirmed the Notes tab's textarea showed the same marker on load — real persistence, not DOM-only. Also clicked the Notes tab's own "Save Notes" button afterward and confirmed it now succeeds ("Session notes saved ✓") where it would previously have 409'd. All test data (`player_decisions`) reverted to `null` via SQL afterward. Zero console errors throughout. Pre-push `new Function()` syntax check clean on every extracted inline script block. Committed and pushed to `anavale-wiki-scc-ux`.

**2026-08-12 (new session — DM Session Command Center UX/IA investigation, 7 of 8 goals, no code changed) — investigated 7 proposed DM Tools features against the real current `dm.html` state before any build. Goal 8 (Claude integration architecture) explicitly out of scope this pass — already decided by the DM separately.**
Per explicit instruction, this was investigation only — 7 parallel research passes plus one dedicated color-collision audit, each grounded in reading the actual current file (not project-knowledge descriptions) and live Supabase schema/data queries, not assumptions. Full findings relayed to the DM in chat; condensed here for continuity since several are real, previously-unknown bugs, not just scoping gaps.
**Color scheme collision (checked before any goal, since it cuts across all of them)**: the DM's proposed new axis — Read Aloud (bright green) / DM Only (bright orange) / NPCs Neutral (medium yellow) / NPCs Hostile (medium red) / NPCs Quest-Related Neutral (bright yellow) / NPCs Quest-Related Hostile (bright red) — has real collisions, not just close hues, with systems already live in `dm.html`: "DM Only" already has two existing colors elsewhere (Field Guide's `.scc-fg-box--yellow` gold `#f0b429`, and the `player_facing` badge's red `#f87171` fallback) — a third color for the same label is a genuine conflict, and the proposed orange specifically collides in hue with Field Guide's *existing* "loot/shop" orange (`#fb923c`, used 6× in the same document). "NPCs (Hostile)" duplicates the existing NPC-relationship "hostile" state, already `#f87171` (`SCC_REL_CYCLE`, `dm.html:11618`). "NPCs (Neutral)" sits directly on top of the app's already-overloaded brand gold (`#f0b429` = caution / teaser / region-health-muted / quest-available / the global UI accent color everywhere). Structurally, NPCs already carry two independent color-coded axes at once (relationship-to-party, and `player_facing` visibility) — layering a third "NPC type" axis on the same entities without a stated design (replace vs. coexist, and if coexisting, which visual channel wins) means a single NPC could need 3-4 simultaneous colors. This needs a real decision before any hex values are finalized, not just a hue tweak.
**Goal 1 (Search+Preview)**: `sccGlobalSearch()`/`sccOpenLookup()` work today against live Supabase data (confirmed not stale-seed data despite a plausible-looking risk from `sccInit()`'s narrower fetch list — a separate DOMContentLoaded sync IIFE at `dm.html:3844` fetches all 10 world tables before the DM can type). **Two real bugs found**: clicking an Organization search result always shows "Entry not found" (`sccOpenLookup`'s `city` branch never includes organizations despite `sccGlobalResultClick` remapping org→city), and clicking an Item result renders nothing at all (`sccOpenLookup` has no `item` branch whatsoever). `REGIONS`/`RELIGIONS`/`SPECIES` are loaded live but never included in search. For "open full page in new tab": the public wiki has real hash routing for most entity types, but **no `#quest` route exists anywhere** in `js/router.js`/`js/wiki.js` — quests have no public page to link to yet — and ~90% of DM-tool content is `player_facing:false`, so most lookups would 404 on the public wiki today. Real, groundable filter fields exist per entity type (documented in chat) — but two existing dropdowns (Content Manager's color_health and item-category filters) already have stale option lists that don't match real data; don't copy those blind into new filter UI.
**Goal 2 (Session Guide vs. Field Guide)**: Field Guide (`scc-fg-*`, `dm.html:3121`) is a real two-part component — a fully dynamic, session-scoped left sidebar (Supabase-driven) and a **100% hardcoded, Session-1-only right-hand document** (one literal HTML block, no template, no session selector). Already has a working 5-color read-aloud/DM-only/lore/comedy/loot box convention, documented in-page. Zero inline popup links exist inside the document text today. Verdict: the ask is an extension of this existing component, not a new feature — but the real gap (multi-session support for the static document) is genuine new build work, not a parameter tweak.
**Goal 3 (Quest Manager)**: search-without-knowing-the-name and per-beat progression UI already fully work (`questsFilter()`, `sccCycleBeatStatus()` + real `quest_progress` table). Affected-entities data exists but renders as inert non-clickable tags. No `suggested_level` column exists anywhere. All 10 real quests are `player_facing:false` and **no public wiki route/renderer for quests exists at all** — the DM/player split has no player-facing half to toggle into yet, a bigger gap than a UI checkbox. Also found: 2 of 10 quests have malformed `stakes` data (free sentences instead of the enum), silently breaking their filter/sort/badge — a data-quality fix worth bundling with any Quest Manager work.
**Goal 4 (Session Setup)**: genuinely flat, single-panel, all 5 entity pickers rendered simultaneously — no existing sub-nav to reorganize, confirmed a real gap. Setup is the single write surface for a shared `session_notes` row that NPCs/Locations/Quests/Field Guide all read from (hub-and-spoke, not siloed) — except `session_scenarios` (jsonb), which has **no editor anywhere in the app**, Supabase-only today, and Combat's creature-add search, which is fully disconnected from Setup's session roster.
**Goal 5 (Note-taking)**: found a real, previously-unknown bug — the Live Session "Quick Note" field (`sccLiveSaveNote()`, `dm.html:10757`) **does not write to Supabase**. It only splices a timestamped line into the DOM value of the "Player Decisions" textarea on the separate Notes tab, unconditionally regardless of what the note is actually about, and is lost if the DM never later clicks "Save Notes" there. `session_notes` has no tag/category column at all. Zero @mention/caret-tracking scaffolding exists anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via full grep) — the three existing predictive-search implementations (`sccGlobalSearch`/`sccLiveSearch`/`sccPickerSearch`) share reusable filter logic but none do caret-aware inline text insertion, so this is real new build work, not an adaptation. `sheet/index.html`'s player-facing Notes tab (tagged, searchable, `character_notes` table) is a solid structural reference for what a real filterable DM notes view could look like.
**Goal 6 (Combat Tracker)**: creature-add is a single free-text name-only search, zero filters today. Of the 6 proposed filter dimensions (race/level/CR/organization/nation/army), **only CR has a real column, and it's populated on just 4 of 52 creatures.** Race/level/organization/nation/army have no corresponding schema at all (nation's closest real analog is `regions[]`, 100% populated); "army" doesn't exist anywhere in the codebase, in any table. NPCs (`world_characters`) aren't in the combat flow at all and have zero mechanical fields (no HP/AC/CR) — unifying NPC+creature filtering needs new schema on `world_characters`, not just a filter UI.
**Goal 7 (Vendor/Shop)**: no mechanism exists anywhere. The Field Guide's "Shop" section is pure static prose meant for the DM to read aloud, zero JS wiring. The closest analog, `sccLootItemSearch()`/`sccAwardItem()`, is a one-way DM-grants-item-free flow — no gold check, no deduction, ever, in either direction (confirmed: no inventory-insert path anywhere touches `gold_cp`, and no inventory-delete anywhere credits it back). `items.cost_gp` is populated on 137/139 real rows, so pricing data itself is usable. Net-new needed: a stock-list entity (nothing links to `pois`' Mira Pont row today), a browse/buy/sell UI (existing catalog-search-and-add pattern is a reasonable starting point), and — the core missing piece — real transaction logic that atomically moves gold and inventory together, which doesn't exist today in either direction.
Not built this session per explicit DM instruction — phased build to follow across multiple future prompts, DM to choose ordering.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, live DM review of the Skills section below) — Replaced the proficient-skill filled dot with a "Prof." text label (`sheet/index.html`).**
DM reviewed the new Skills section live and flagged the filled-vs-outlined dot distinction (proficient vs. flat-bonus) as not intuitive, and asked for two changes: replace the filled dot with a "Prof." text label, and show the flat-bonus amount as a number in parens next to the total for flat-bonus skills.
`abilitySkillsInnerHtml()`: proficient rows now render `<span class="cs-ab-skill-prof-label">Prof.</span>` in place of the marker dot (small caps, gold, bold) — the ring dot for flat-bonus rows and the hidden dot for neither are untouched, only the proficient state's marker changed. Wrapped both marker types in a fixed-width `.cs-ab-skill-marker` (26px) so the name column still lines up whether a row shows "Prof." or a dot. For flat-bonus rows, the total string now appends the real stored `skill_bonuses[].amount` in parens (`bonusesArr.find()` replacing the earlier `.some()` existence check, since the amount is needed for display) rather than hardcoding "+1" — the mechanic is currently always a flat +1 per `PAST_EFFECTS`, but this reads the real value instead of assuming it always will be.
Verified on Kael (Athletics: `+2 (+1)`, flat state; Animal Handling/Perception/Survival: `Prof.` label, `+5`, no parenthetical) and Hana (her real overlap case, Insight: `Prof.` label, `+5`, correctly no parenthetical — confirms the flat bonus stays fully superseded and invisible on proficient rows, not just hidden visually). Zero console errors. `new Function()` syntax check clean. Pushed live.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, after the Speaks-color fix below) — Added a Skills section to the Ability Scores tab (`sheet/index.html`). Investigated first per explicit DM instruction, reported findings, built after DM decisions on 2 open questions.**
**Investigation (Step 1)**: (a) Checked Figma for a Skills frame under Ability Scores — pulled `get_metadata` on the file's page tree, found the desktop (`51:1663`) and mobile (`51:1668`) Ability Scores frames, then `get_screenshot`'d both directly. Confirmed neither contains anything beyond the header + the 6 ability-score cards already built — no Skills section exists anywhere in the file, on any tab. Same situation as Special Effects: flagged clearly rather than guessing at a design that isn't there. (b) Confirmed `character_sheet.proficiency_bonus` is a real stored integer column (via `list_tables`) — populated `2` on all 3 characters (level 1, matches standard scaling), seeded once at first sheet creation (`sheet/index.html`, hardcoded `2` on insert) and never read anywhere in the codebase since. Recommended reading `SHEET.proficiency_bonus` directly rather than deriving it from `ch.level` via a hardcoded scaling table, since it's already the DM's own editable value (same pattern as `hp_max_modifier`) — confirmed correct by DM. (c) Checked real data for overlap between `ch.skills` (full proficiency) and `PAST_EFFECTS`' flat +1 `skill_bonuses`, rather than reasoning it out abstractly: Kael Evander is proficient in *and* has a flat +1 on both Animal Handling and Survival; Hana Sable is proficient in *and* has a flat +1 on Insight; Visenya Oryn has no overlap. No existing code anywhere combines the two. Recommended proficiency supersede the flat +1 (based on the existing "not full proficiency, **but still useful**" tooltip copy implying the flat bonus's purpose is specifically to help skills you *lack* proficiency in) — DM confirmed with a stronger, decisive rationale: the 2024 PHB's stacking rule (same-target bonuses don't add; the higher one applies), which lands on the identical outcome since proficiency bonus (≥+2) always beats the flat +1.
**Build (Step 2)**: new `ABILITY_SKILLS` constant — all 18 skills mapped to their parent ability (fixed D&D rules, not derived from data; CON gets `[]`). New `abilitySkillsInnerHtml(ab, modNum, profBonus, ch)` renders one ability's skill list (or a "No skills" state for CON) — each skill shows name + `SKILL_TIPS` tooltip (verbatim, no new copy) + total bonus (`modNum + profBonus` if in `ch.skills`, `modNum + 1` if not proficient but present in `ch.skill_bonuses`, else `modNum` alone) + one of three visually distinct states (proficient: filled gold dot, bold name; flat: gold-ring dot, normal weight; neither: no dot, muted name — new `.cs-ab-skill--proficient/--flat/--none` CSS, no existing classes reused since nothing like this existed). Rendered inside each `.cs-ab-card`, below the existing modifier line, with `id="cs-ab-skills-{ab}"` per card. Because `buildAbilityScoresHtml()` runs before `SHEET` (holding the real `proficiency_bonus`) finishes loading — same ordering gap `renderHeaderHP()` already works around — added `renderAbilitySkills()`, called from `renderLivePlay()`, which re-renders all 6 skill blocks in place once `SHEET` is available; initial render falls back to `2` (matches the real seed default). The old flat "Proficient skills: X, Y, Z" comma-list (`cs-story-block`/`cs-story-row`) is fully removed — the grouped list covers the same information plus bonus math and flat-bonus skills it never showed, so nothing was left rendering redundantly.
**Verification**: live on all 3 real characters via the local dev server, both breakpoints. Kael (1600px): Animal Handling/Perception/Survival correctly render `cs-ab-skill--proficient` at `+5` (WIS mod +3, prof +2) — confirmed via DOM read, not just visual, that the two real overlap skills (Animal Handling, Survival) render as proficient-only, not stacked (`+5`, not `+6`); Athletics correctly renders `cs-ab-skill--flat` at `+2` (STR mod +1, flat +1); everything else renders `cs-ab-skill--none` at ability-mod-only. To confirm the async refresh path (not just the initial fallback), temporarily set Kael's real `proficiency_bonus` to `3` via SQL, reloaded, confirmed the 3 proficient skills recomputed live to `+6` via `renderAbilitySkills()`, then reverted to `2` via a follow-up SQL read. Hana (her real overlap case, Insight): confirmed `+5` (WIS mod +3, prof +2), not stacked. Visenya (no overlap): confirmed all 3 states render correctly with no false overlaps. Mobile (375px): 2-per-row wrap, "No skills" state renders cleanly on CON, zero console errors, `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth` reads 1px (sub-pixel rounding, not a real overflow). `new Function()` syntax check clean on the extracted inline script. Committed (`ba13af1`), pushed after DM review; see the "Prof." label follow-up above for the one legibility fix that came out of that review.

**2026-08-12 (later same day, live DM review continued) — Fixed the Speaks block's language name text color (`sheet/index.html`).**
DM flagged live on Hana Sable's sheet (screenshot from `carlosknightiii.github.io`): "Jugabi Canopy" read as blue text next to its own green rune glyph, and asked for the text to match "the color of the region it is from." Checked the data model before assuming a fix: `regions` has no hex color column at all (only `color_health`, a status word — Dire/Excellent/Faded/Good/Moderate, confirmed via `information_schema`), so there's no literal region-color field to join against. `languages.color` is the real signal — confirmed via a full table read that it already tracks each language's origin region visually (`jugabi-canopy` `#4a9a3a` green, `nombi-frost` `#2266b8` blue, `sohot-old` `#c8882a` gold, `caparian-deep` `#2a7a3a` green, `conclave-script` `#6a3aaa` purple, `pre-partition` `#8a6a2a` brown) — this is what the glyph already used. Root cause confirmed live via `getComputedStyle()` before touching anything: `.cs-mi-speaks-glyph` got `LANGUAGE_ROW.color` as an inline style, `.cs-mi-speaks-name` never did, staying on a fixed `color: var(--blue)` CSS rule regardless of language. Fix: `renderSpeaksBlock()` now applies the same `LANGUAGE_ROW.color` inline to both spans; the CSS rule's `var(--blue)` is now just an unreachable fallback for the (currently nonexistent) case of a character with no `language_extra` row resolved. Verified on both Hana (Jugabi Canopy, glyph+name both `rgb(74,154,58)`) and Kael (Nombi Frost, both `rgb(34,102,184)`) via computed style, not just visual read. Zero console errors.

**2026-08-12 — Special Effects panel internal styling rebuilt against a fresh Figma pull, node 51:2260 (`css/tokens.css`, `sheet/index.html`).**
Ask was explicit: re-pull the node fresh rather than build from the request's own bullet list. Pulled `get_design_context` on 51:2260 (file `P0tcVgf02XeI13Tz0TtRaX`, same file as the rest of this tab) and read the real "Special Effects" subtree (`85:1765`/`91:1440`/`91:1449`/`91:1457`) directly — this caught a structural mismatch the bullet list didn't call out: Figma has **one shared bordered box** with each equipped item as a "row" inside it (Figma's own `data-name="row"` on the wrapping div, border-bottom-2 on that same div), not the per-item `.cs-story-card` cards the panel was originally built with. Rebuilt to match: new `.cs-effect-panel` is the single box (bg `--blue-700`, 1px `--blue-300` border, `--radius` 10px, `--char-ov-gap-s` 26px padding — all pre-existing tokens, exact hex/px matches confirmed against Figma's own variable dump, zero guessing); each item is a `.cs-effect-item` with the bottom divider (2px `--blue-300`) applied once per item, not per fact-line — confirmed against Figma's real DOM (`border-b-2` sits on the row wrapping name+mechanical+flavor together), and confirmed both items in Figma's own 2-item example carry the divider including the last one, matching the ask's explicit "including the last row" instruction.

The ask said "check current size against this and correct if smaller" (size was already 26px) but the actual gap was font-family, caught by re-pulling rather than trusting the bullet list's framing.
**Mechanical rows** (`.cs-effect-mech`): sparkle icon (`&#10024;`, HTML entity per the file's C2 no-literal-emoji rule) at `--char-ov-h3` (32px) bold, text in `--gold` (`#f0b429`) at a new `--char-ov-pl` token (24px, added to `tokens.css` — no existing token matched 24px; named to mirror the existing `--char-ov-h3/h4/h5` convention and mapped directly to Figma's `page/fonts/pl`). Replaces the old red-left-border boxed treatment entirely — DM's explicit correction, not a refinement of it.
**Flavor rows** (`.cs-effect-flavor`): book icon (`&#128214;`) at the same `--char-ov-pl` (24px, matches Figma's icon sizing exactly), 13px gap, italic text in `--text` at `--char-ov-h5` (18px, reused rather than adding a redundant `--page/fonts/pm` token since the pixel value already exists under that name).
**Height**: Figma's `h-[277px]` on the "other" column is the canvas size of its 2-item example, not a real cap — implemented as `min-height:277px` on `.cs-effect-panel` per the ask's explicit correction of Figma's literal fixed value, confirmed to actually grow (not clip/scroll) by testing 3 real items including one deliberately long flavor sentence: panel grew from the 277px floor to a measured 405px, `overflow` never engaged.
**Data model note, not a design gap**: Figma's 2-item mock shows exactly one mechanical line per item; this panel's real data can carry several (`magic_bonus`/`grants_advantage[]`/`grants_proficiency[]`/`equipped_effect_mechanical` are all independent). Kept the existing one-`.cs-effect-mech`-row-per-fact behavior (pre-existing, not part of this ask) — Figma's divider-per-item structure supports this cleanly since the border lives on the outer `.cs-effect-item`, not on each fact line.
**Test data**: Kael's Longbow already carried real DM-set effect text from an earlier session (not touched). Added two temporary items for full coverage per the ask — his equipped Braided Cord Ring got a mechanical-only `Invisibility` line (Kael doesn't own a literal "Ring of Invisibility," so this simulates Figma's own example against a real equipped item rather than inventing a fake catalog entry) and his Leather Armor got a mechanical line plus a deliberately long flavor sentence to stress-test the "grows as needed" requirement. **2026-08-10 — Fixed a real double-equip gap in `selectEquipItem()` (`sheet/index.html`), surfaced live right after the mechanical/cosmetic-split retirement below. DM reported Kael showing the same Braided Cord Ring in both Right Ring and Left Ring simultaneously.**
**Investigated before touching anything, per DM instruction.** Confirmed via SQL: Kael owns exactly one `braided-cord-ring` inventory row (not a migration duplicate) — but `character_sheet.equipped` had `ring_left` and `ring_right` both pointing at that same catalog id, meaning the one physical row was referenced from two slots at once. Root cause read from the code, not guessed: `getEquipCandidates()` lists an owned item as a slot candidate purely by category/`equip_slot` match, with no check for whether that same row is already equipped somewhere else; `selectEquipItem()`'s mutual-exclusion logic only clears *sibling keys within one slot's own `keys` array* (e.g. `hand_left` vs `shield`, both backing the Left Hand doll position) — it has no concept of "this item is already the value of a completely different slot." Confirmed this isn't ring-specific: `handL`/`handR` both accept category `'weapon'` (two separate slots, same collision shape), so a single owned one-handed weapon had the identical exposure — just not yet hit live. Every other slot has a unique `accessorySlotKey`, so no other pair collides.
**Fix, scoped exactly per DM instruction — same physical row, not same category**: added one general check in `selectEquipItem()`, keyed off the inventory row's own `equipped_slot` column (which slot *that specific row* currently occupies) rather than the catalog id shared by `equipped[key]` values. If the row being equipped already has a *different* `equipped_slot` than the target, that old key gets cleared as part of the same action — the item moves, it doesn't duplicate. Guarded with `equipped[priorSlotKey] === equipKey` before clearing, specifically because `unequipSlot()` never resets a row's `equipped_slot` column back to null (a separate, pre-existing gap, not fixed here) — without that guard, a stale column value could falsely clear an unrelated key that was never actually holding this item. Not hardcoded to rings/hands — works off any inventory row id match across any two keys, so it also covers the pair that hasn't been exercised yet.
**Confirmed does NOT affect Visenya's legitimate case** (two separate owned rings, one in each ring slot): the check is keyed off the literal inventory row id, and her two rings are two distinct rows with two distinct `equipped_slot` values — reopening either of her ring popovers does nothing (no `selectEquipItem()` call happens on view), and her state was confirmed byte-identical before and after.
Reset Kael's `ring_right` back to empty via direct SQL (should never have been set — he owns one ring, it belongs in `ring_left` per the original migration). Verified live end-to-end: Kael's Right Ring popover correctly shows "Nothing equipped" with the one owned ring listed as a legitimate candidate (not a false "already equipped" block — the fix allows selecting it, it just moves instead of duplicating); clicked it, confirmed `ring_left` cleared and `ring_right` populated, then reversed the click to move it back, confirmed clean round-trip. Visenya's two-ring state re-verified unchanged after opening (not clicking) her Right Ring popover. Hana's Quarterstaff (one owned one-handed weapon) used as the hand-slot equivalent test — moved Right→Left (confirmed `hand_right` cleared, `hand_left` populated, screenshotted), then moved back, confirmed restored to the original migrated baseline. All three characters' `character_sheet.equipped` confirmed via a final SQL read to exactly match their pre-fix-testing correct state. Zero console errors throughout.

**2026-08-10 — Investigated why Kael Evander has a Gold ring equipped (25gp) against a 10gp starting budget (Greywitnessed). Not a bug — logged here, no code changed.**
Checked all three possible explanations before concluding: (1) Stage 4 shopping overspend is a genuine hard block in `js/character.js` (`validateStage(4)` computes `getStartingGold() - calcGoldSpent()`, blocks `goNext()`/`jumpToStage()` on negative remainder) — not a soft warning, ruled out. (2) The ring's price (`cost_gp: 25` in `STATIC_OPTION_COSTS`) hasn't changed since May 27 2026, before Kael's July 6 creation, and isn't DM-Tools-editable at all (it's hardcoded in creator JS, not a Supabase `items` row) — ruled out. (3) The validation itself isn't buggy either — the ring alone (25gp) exceeds Greywitnessed's entire 10gp budget, so it's mathematically impossible for it to have passed the real Stage 4 hard block under any combination of other purchases. Real explanation: `appearance_data` (which is what actually stores "a gold ring") can only be written from two places — Stage 4's validated shopping flow (ruled out above) or the character sheet's own "Currently Wearing" cosmetic picker (`saveAppearance()`/`selectWearingOption()`), which has zero cost or ownership validation by design, since post-creation appearance edits are meant to be free. DM Tools never touches `appearance_data` at all (confirmed via grep), so that's not the path either. Conclusion: two intentionally separate systems — a one-time budget-gated shop and a permanently free-form cosmetic picker — both working as built.
**Flagging forward, not acting**: the DM confirmed a bigger change is planned (not yet built) requiring cosmetic items to be actually owned in inventory before they can be equipped, matching how weapons/armor already work via `getEquipCandidates()`. That would make this exact scenario structurally impossible going forward. Worth remembering when next touching `APPEARANCE_OPTIONS`/`STATIC_OPTION_COSTS`/`renderPopoverCosmeticRow()` — check whether that ownership-gating has landed before assuming the current free-form static-list behavior (hat/cloak/ring/necklace/earrings/shoes/lower/top, all unfiltered by real inventory) is still current. The hand-slot branch already partially does this (weapon-name cosmetic options are sourced from real inventory rows) — the other 8 option lists don't yet.

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: the "Currently Wearing" cosmetic picker (`renderPopoverCosmeticRow()`) replaced its native `<select>` with a clickable thumbnail list matching the "Currently Equipped" mechanical picker's own row style — one shared function, so this fixes all 10 doll slots plus both rings at once. Same session, right after the ring-tile conversion below.**
Reused the mechanical picker's existing markup/CSS as the base rather than inventing new styling, per the ask: `.cs-equip-results`/`.cs-equip-result`/`.cs-equip-thumb`/`.cs-equip-result-info`/`.cs-equip-result-name` (and, for the currently-selected row, `.cs-equip-result-flag`, reusing the same "Also equipped: X" flag language for a new "Current" flag) — zero new CSS classes for the row itself, only a new `buildWearingResultThumb()` (cosmetic art has no category to fall back to the way `buildEquipResultThumb()`'s armor/weapon/shield icons do, so it falls back to a plain "—" swatch instead) and the now-dead `.cs-wearing-select` rule removed.
**No search box added** — checked real option counts first rather than guessing: the static lists run 5–18 entries (`top` is the longest at 18, every other list is 5–8), hand slots are bounded by the player's actual weapon inventory (typically 1–3), and the existing `.cs-equip-results` scroll cap (`max-height:320px`) the mechanical picker already uses handles the longest case the same way it already handles longer mechanical-item lists — no new component needed.
**Real escaping gap found and fixed, not a hypothetical**: switching from `<select onchange>` (reads `.value` off the DOM element) to `<div onclick="fn('...')">` (embeds the option's value as a literal JS-string argument) introduced a genuine risk the old picker never had — three real `top` option values contain apostrophes (`"monk's gi"`, `"traveller's coat"`, `"ranger's mail"`), and hand-slot options can be arbitrary player-typed custom item names. Added `escJsString()` (backslash then single-quote) and ran every embedded value through it — confirmed live by actually selecting "Monk's gi" through the real UI (not just eyeballing the generated HTML) and checking it saved correctly with no console error, since a broken escape here fails silently as a JS syntax error inside the inline handler, not a visible crash.
**Verified all three requested cases, both breakpoints**: Left Ring (7 options, no mechanical section at all — confirmed the previous round's `cs-equip-mechanical-section` hide/`cs-wearing-section-solo` still holds with the new list markup inside it), Back (both sections present together, mechanical unaffected), and a slot with an effectively-empty cosmetic list — no real character currently has zero weapons in inventory, so this was tested by temporarily clearing the live `INVENTORY` array in the browser (not Supabase — confirmed via `getEquipCandidates('handR').length === 0`) to exercise the true zero-candidate path (list collapses to just the "— none —" row), then restoring the array from a saved copy afterward. Clicking a real option (Signet ring, then back to Braided cord ring) end-to-end confirmed: modal closes on select (matching the mechanical picker's own close-on-equip behavior), the tile updates immediately, and the write lands in Supabase — checked via a follow-up SQL read that `ring_left`/`ring_right`/`hand_right`/`cloak` all match their pre-test values exactly, nothing left mutated. Zero new console errors; every real option's thumbnail loaded a real image (confirmed via network log, not assumed) except the same pre-existing, already-documented `appearance/longbow.webp` 404 (hand slots have no weapon art at all, graceful "—" fallback, unrelated to this change).

**2026-08-10 — `sheet/index.html`: ring selectors converted from plain `<select>` dropdowns into real slot tiles matching the other 10 gear slots. Same session, right after the weapon-slot changes below.**
`renderRingsRow()` rebuilt to emit the exact same `.cs-equip-slot-wrap`/`.cs-equip-slot-box`/`.cs-equip-slot-label`/`.cs-equip-slot-name` markup the 10 doll slots use (new `renderRingSlot()`) instead of `.cs-ring-item`/`.cs-ring-select` — clickable, opens the same popover via `openSlotPopover(ring.key)`. `RING_SLOTS` labels shortened to match the other slots' terse style ("Right Ring"/"Left Ring", was "Right Hand Ring"/"Left Hand Ring") — used both on the tile and as the popover title now that rings open a real popover instead of just an inline `<select>`. Dropped the now-fully-dead `.cs-ring-item`/`.cs-ring-label`/`.cs-ring-select` CSS (confirmed via grep no longer referenced anywhere).
**Sizing had a real trap, caught before shipping, not after**: `.cs-equip-slot-wrap`'s width comes from `cqi` (container query units) relative to `.cs-equip-doll`'s own `container-type:inline-size` — but `#cs-ring-row` is a *sibling* of `#cs-equipped-block` (i.e. of the doll), not a descendant, so it can't inherit that containment. Per spec, `cqi` with no qualifying container resolves to 0, which would have silently pinned every ring tile to its `clamp()` floor (~50px) forever, regardless of viewport — visually smaller than the other 10 slots at any width above the floor, the opposite of "matching." Fixed by giving `.cs-ring-row` the *identical* sizing formula as `.cs-equip-doll` (`width:100%; max-width:61.08rem; container-type:inline-size`) rather than trying to share containment — both derive their width from the same `.cs-inv-top-col` parent, so their `cqi` math always resolves to the same absolute px at any viewport. Verified via `getBoundingClientRect()`, not assumed: ring tile and doll tile boxes measured pixel-identical at both 1440px desktop (128.7×131.0px) and 375px mobile (70.4×71.7px, the mobile touch-target floor).
**Popover — rings skip the "Currently Equipped" section entirely**, not just its search box (the existing empty-candidates fix from an earlier round hides only the search input, for slots that *can* be mechanical but currently aren't — Back with nothing equipped). Rings can never be mechanical (`items.equip_slot` has never had a ring value), so this is a different case. Added `id="cs-equip-mechanical-section"` to that block's static markup; `openSlotPopover()` now branches on whether the target is a doll slot or a ring (new `ringSlotByKey()` lookup) and toggles that section's `display` accordingly, skipping `renderPopoverMechanicalRow()`/`renderEquipResults()` entirely for rings rather than calling them into an empty state.
**Real bug caught during this, not shipped**: the `.cs-slot-popover-section + .cs-slot-popover-section` adjacent-sibling CSS rule (the divider between the two sections) still matched `#cs-wearing-section` even with the mechanical section `display:none`, since CSS sibling combinators walk the DOM tree, not the render/paint tree — `display:none` removes an element from paint, not from the DOM the selector matches against. Caught live via a fresh screenshot before writing it off as done (would have shown a border/margin floating above "Currently Wearing" with nothing above it). Fixed with a new `#cs-wearing-section.cs-wearing-section-solo` override (ID+class specificity beats the two-class combinator rule), added/removed alongside the mechanical section's `display` toggle.
**Cosmetic picker reused, not rebuilt**: both rings now route through the exact same `renderPopoverCosmeticRow()`/`onWearingChange()` every other slot uses — required generalizing two lookups, not adding a parallel path. `SLOT_APPEARANCE_KEY` gained `ring_right`/`ring_left` self-mappings (their doll "area" and their `appearance_data` storage key are the same string, unlike e.g. `handL`→`hand_left`). Both rings share ONE options list (`APPEARANCE_OPTIONS.ring`) despite having two distinct storage keys, which the existing `appKey`-doubles-as-options-key convention couldn't express — added a small `APPEARANCE_OPTIONS_KEY` map (`ring_right`/`ring_left` → `'ring'`) and changed the one options-lookup line to check it first, falling through to `appKey` itself unchanged for every other slot (verified: 7 real ring options loaded in the popover, matching the shared list, not a truncated/wrong one).
Verification: live on Kael Evander (real Gold ring / Braided cord ring on both hands) at 1440px desktop and 375px mobile — tiles render the correct ring art/name, popover shows only "Right Ring"/"Left Ring" + "Currently Wearing" + its cosmetic explainer + a real 7-option select, zero "Currently Equipped" section, zero orphaned border. Cross-checked a normal slot (Back) immediately after closing a ring popover to confirm no regression — mechanical section correctly restores (`display:block`, "Nothing equipped", real search box). `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth === 0` at both breakpoints. Zero new console errors (ring art itself loaded clean — `gold-ring.webp`/`braided-cord-ring.webp` both 200 OK; the only 404 is the same pre-existing, already-documented `appearance/longbow.webp` fallback, unrelated).

2. **Two-handed mirror image**: the locked "other hand" slot (`.cs-equip-slot-locked`, existing class, reused verbatim — still just `opacity:0.4`, no new grayscale/filter invented) now renders the two-handed weapon's own image instead of an empty box, with no name or damage line (that text only belongs on the hand actually holding it). Replaced the hardcoded check with a new `detectTwoHandedLock()` that checks both hands and returns `{activeArea, lockedArea, item, lockedKeys}` — `lockedKeys` is `['hand_left','shield']` when Right Hand is active (Left Hand's doll position has two backing keys) or just `['hand_right']` when Left Hand is active (Right Hand only ever has one). `autoUnequipOffHandForTwoHanded()` now takes `keys` as a parameter instead of hardcoding `['hand_left','shield']`, so the same function clears whichever side is actually locked.

DM supplied exact structure/geometry from the pull directly (flex row, gap-10 items-end justify-center; a Left Hand slot at gap-16 beside a 4-slot Head/Chest/Arms/Feet column, mirrored on the right with Neck/Earrings/Back/Legs + Right Hand; each stacked slot's 177.249px row bottom-justifies its 140.691x143.249 frame + label; silhouette 362.553x759.268 centered between the two groups) — re-pulled it myself before writing any code rather than building from the description alone, confirmed it matched verbatim.
**Silhouette asset check (done first, per explicit instruction not to assume)**: confirmed `equipped-gear-silhouete.png` is the DM's already-replaced version, not the original — its mtime (this session, after the last commit touching it) and its working-tree bytes (128,721) differ from HEAD's committed bytes (181,525) per `git diff --stat`, and its pixel dimensions (363x760) already match the Figma spec almost exactly. Left the image itself untouched.
**Structure**: old grid-template-areas layout (max-width:480px, silhouette absolutely overlaid via grid-column span + object-fit:contain) replaced with real nested flex markup mirroring the Figma node one-to-one — `.cs-equip-doll` > two `.cs-equip-doll-side` groups (each a flanking hand slot + a `.cs-equip-doll-col` of 4 `.cs-equip-slot-row`s) flanking `.cs-equip-doll-silhouette`, all `align-items:flex-end` for the shared bottom baseline the DM described. `renderEquipped()`/`renderEquippedSlot()` rewritten to build this nesting explicitly (using the existing `equipSlotByArea()` lookup, called earlier in the file now — safe, it's a hoisted function declaration) instead of relying on CSS `grid-area` placement; the `grid-area` inline styles are gone entirely. Slot order unchanged, per the DM's explicit instruction — only sizing/spacing/anchoring moved.
**Fluid sizing, not raw px**: build spec (below, then Aug 9 entry above) already flags that hardcoding Figma's canvas px onto a differently-shaped container was exactly what broke the silhouette twice before. Used CSS container query units (`container-type:inline-size` on `.cs-equip-doll`, sized via `width:100%; max-width:61.08rem` off its own flex parent so there's no circular sizing dependency) — every descendant dimension, including heights, is a `cqi` percentage of the doll's own resolved width, so the whole layout scales as one unit exactly like Figma's proportions instead of one fixed breakpoint. `clamp()` floors stop things shrinking below a usable size.
**Real bug caught during verification, not shipped**: first pass added `flex-wrap:wrap` on `.cs-equip-doll` as a "safety net." Since the cqi percentages are tuned to sum to ~100% of the container by construction, this landed within a sub-pixel of the wrap threshold at common widths and browsers rounded one side group onto its own line below the item table — confirmed live via `getBoundingClientRect()` (the two side groups' rects were ~700px apart vertically) before catching it, not from a visual glance. Fixed by switching to `nowrap` and instead sizing the `clamp()` floors low enough (~35.8% of reference size, ~350px total doll width) to guarantee fit within this column's own pre-existing 358px architectural minimum (`.cs-inv-top-col { min-width: 358px }`, unrelated pre-existing CSS, not touched) — worked through the actual flex-wrap/flex-grow arithmetic of the shared Equipped-Gear/Currency row to find the real narrowest case (~397px column width around an ~850px browser window, currency sharing the row without wrapping) rather than assuming the mobile breakpoint was the only risk.
**Mobile (<=600px, existing DM-approved deviation, unchanged in principle)**: silhouette still hides — the real Figma mobile frame reuses this same desktop component with no mobile layout of its own (a gap already flagged in `docs/inventory-tab-build-spec.md`). Added a mobile-only override raising the slot-size floor for a better touch target now that the silhouette's gone and there's room to spare, rather than inheriting the tight ~350px-total floor sized for the silhouette-visible case.
**Label styling**: color changed to `--white` (was `--char-text-faint`, a muted gray) and size changed to a `clamp()` capped at the existing `--char-ov-h5` token (18px) — both explicit in the DM's spec ("Cinzel Decorative Bold, 18px, white"). Left the existing uppercase transform/letter-spacing alone — not mentioned in the ask, and consistent with the Inventory tab's other section headers (EQUIPPED GEAR/CURRENCY/CARRYING all render uppercase the same way).
Verification: JS syntax validated via `new Function()` on the extracted inline script block. Checked live via the local dev server (started fresh this session — `.claude/launch.json` only attaches to an already-running server, wasn't running) on Kael Evander (real leather armor/longbow/worn boots etc.) and Hana Sable (no mechanical items, but real cosmetic-only values on Back/Right Hand/Legs — a better edge-case test than a truly-blank character, confirmed the cosmetic-vs-mechanical badge distinction still renders correctly post-rebuild) at both 1440px desktop and 375px mobile. `getBoundingClientRect()`-verified the bottom-alignment and exact 10px gaps between groups and silhouette, and `document.body.scrollWidth - window.innerWidth === 0` (no horizontal overflow) at all four combinations. Zero new console errors — the only 404s (`appearance/longbow.webp`, `appearance/quarterstaff.webp`) are the pre-existing, already-documented weapon-cosmetic-art-doesn't-exist fallback path, unrelated to this change.

**2026-08-09 — `sheet/index.html`: fixed a real orphaned-tooltip bug (condition pill's own remove button); gave the Moral Alignment card the shared 5-alignments illustration as its thumbnail. Commits `9d0660e`, `c1c808b`, same session as the two regression-fix batches above.**
DM reported a stuck tooltip bubble screenshot (hover text for "Invisible" still on screen after interacting with conditions) plus, mid-investigation, a second report that the Moral Alignment card's "Pragmatic" value has no icon/thumbnail like its sibling cards do.
**Tooltip bug — root cause found via D2, fixed in one shot per D3**: `wireTooltip()` shows a bubble on `mouseenter` and only removes it on `mouseleave` from the source element, both listeners bound once per element. The condition pill's "×" button lives *inside* the pill span that carries `data-tip` — clicking it never fires `mouseleave` (the mouse never leaves the pill's bounds to reach a child), and the click's handler (`removeCondition()` → `saveSheet()` → `renderConditions()`) immediately replaces the whole conditions row via `innerHTML`, destroying the pill without the event that would have cleaned up its bubble ever firing. The bubble, parented to `document.body` (not the pill), is orphaned permanently. Fixed centrally in `wireAllTooltips()` — which every re-render on the page already calls immediately after rebuilding its DOM — by sweeping and removing any stray `.cs-tooltip` elements before rewiring. Reasoned this is safe with a single mouse cursor: a legitimately-open tooltip on some *other*, unrelated element would already have received its own `mouseleave` before any click could reach a different part of the page, so the only case where a bubble survives to see this sweep is exactly the bug case (element destroyed while still hovered). Verified by scripting the exact repro (`mouseenter` then `.click()` on the pill's own remove button, no `mouseleave` in between) — bubble count went 1→0 across the click — and separately confirmed normal hover-then-actually-leave behavior is untouched (1→0 on a real `mouseleave` too, on the AC badge). Test mutation (removing then re-adding "Invisible" on Kael) reverted and confirmed via a follow-up SQL read.
**"Pragmatic" thumbnail — D7 correction**: first reported this as an unresolved asset gap ("no per-alignment icon exists, needs a DM decision") after confirming `character.html`'s 5 `.char-alignment-card` elements have no `data-img`/`background-image`/icon of any kind, and that the only alignment-related asset in the repo, `assets/images/img-alignment.webp`, is one combined illustration of all 5 alignments together rather than 5 separate per-value icons. That factual read was correct, but the conclusion was wrong — the DM corrected it on the spot: they'd already decided this combined image *is* the intended thumbnail, used as-is (same image regardless of which of the 5 alignments the character has), not cropped per-value like Gigglegloom Affinity/Home Region/Background's real per-value icons. Fixed per D7 (stopped, corrected the record, then wrote one fix): `alignCard = storyCard(...)` in `renderBackgroundCardsHtml()` now passes `'../assets/images/img-alignment.webp'` as `imgSrc` unconditionally, no per-alignment branching. Verified the image loads (`naturalWidth:1024`, `complete:true`) and renders inside the existing 75×75 `object-fit:cover` thumbnail frame correctly on Kael, zero console errors.

Four more items from the same live-page-vs-mockup comparison as the batch-1 entry above, fixed and verified as a separate batch per the DM's explicit request (screenshot before/after each item, commit each batch separately). Used the `git stash` before/after pattern (established earlier this session) to get a clean "before" state for screenshots after realizing the first edit (section titles) had already landed before any screenshot was taken — stashed, reloaded or Kael to capture "before," restored the stashed change by hand (a plain `git stash pop` refused because the working tree already had the batch's other 3 edits in it by that point — re-applied the 2-line diff manually from `git stash show -p` instead of fighting the pop, then dropped the stash) rather than losing it.
**Section titles**: `.cs-ov-acc-title` (shared by all three "Your Story" headings) was `font-size: var(--text-4xl)` = 48px — should have been 32px per spec. Changed to `var(--char-ov-h3)` (already a 2rem/32px token, used elsewhere for exactly this kind of heading) and added explicit `font-weight:700` (Cinzel Decorative only ships as 700 anyway, but making it explicit removes any doubt). Also removed a now-obsolete mobile override (`font-size: 2.25rem` at max-width:600px) that existed only to shrink the old 48px down for narrow screens — at the new 32px default it's already smaller than that override was, so keeping it would have made mobile headings *bigger* than desktop.
**Your Background card stretching**: `.cs-story-cards` (the shared flex row for both Your Background's and Your Past's card grids) had no `align-items` set, so it defaulted to `stretch` — every card in a row was forced to the height of the tallest one, which is why Gigglegloom Affinity/Home Region/Moral Alignment (a few lines of text) were rendering with a huge dead zone at the bottom to match the Background card's real height (it has a whole Benefits box). Fixed with one line, `align-items: flex-start`, on the shared class — confirmed via `getBoundingClientRect()` that the four Your Background cards now render at four different real heights (204/228/208/497px on Kael) instead of all matching the tallest.
**Tooltip icons**: `.cs-ab-tip` (the shared "?" class used by the Background card's Benefits box, every Your Past card's Benefits box, and separately the Ability Scores tab's per-ability tooltips — three call sites, not two) was plain gold text with no circle/background at all, despite looking superficially like it might be styled. Rebuilt as a real 13×13px circle (`border-radius:50%`, `background:var(--gold)`, `color:var(--blue-800)`, bold, centered) — both `--gold` (#f0b429) and `--blue-800` (#0a0a0f) already matched the DM's spec values exactly, no new tokens needed. Verified via a DOM sweep, not just one card: all 15 instances on Kael's Your Story tab (Background + 5 Your Past cards with benefits) render identically, and separately confirmed the Ability Scores tab's 6 tooltips (one per ability) picked up the same fix automatically since it's the same shared class.
**"Why You Left" quest label**: `.cs-story-quest-label` had `color: var(--gold)` — a real mismatch with the spec, which reserves gold (`--currency-gold-coin`, a different token, used by `.cs-story-benefit-label` for "Benefits:") for Benefits-box labels only. Changed to `var(--text)` (#e8e8f0, matches spec exactly) — "Personal quest:" now renders as bold body-color text, matching the rest of that card, not mistaken for a second Benefits-style label.
Verification: DOM-measured (not just visually eyeballed) on Kael Evander — computed `font-size`/`font-weight`/`font-family` on all three section titles (32px/700/Cinzel Decorative), computed `color`/`font-weight` on the quest label (rgb(232,232,240)/700), computed size/bg/color/radius on 5 sample tooltip icons (13×13/gold/dark/50%), and real per-card heights in the Your Background row. Full spot-check repeated on Visenya Oryn (different background/past answers — Flamerage/Sohot/Measured vs. Kael's Featherflow/Nombi/Pragmatic) confirming none of the fixes are Kael-specific. Zero console errors on both characters throughout.

**2026-08-09 — `sheet/index.html`: fixed 5 MainInfo regressions found by comparing the live page directly against the real Figma header (node `51:1385`) — duplicate logo, AC badge not stacking, HP bar too small, Speaks block wrong structure, tab bar not full width. Commit `0a81614`.**
DM flagged these against a mockup (no image was actually attached to the request — pulled the real Figma frame instead, same source used for the ground-truth rebuild the entry above this one). Loaded the live page (Kael Evander, local dev server on :8791) before touching any code, per D2/C1, and confirmed all 5 were real, not stale-cache artifacts from last session's `tokens.css` caching issue (used a `?cb=` query-string bust each reload).
**Duplicate logo**: root cause was a deliberate choice from the ground-truth rebuild session, recorded in that session's own comment ("Duplicates the sticky nav's logo deliberately — this is a real Figma component distinct from the site-wide nav bar, not a redundant copy of it"). The DM's read of the mockup overrides that — the real Figma frame for this page shows exactly one logo, in the PageTitle component, with no separate nav-bar logo at all. Removed `.cs-nav-logo`/`.cs-nav-title` (markup + CSS) entirely; `.cs-nav` now holds only "← Return to Wiki", right-aligned (`justify-content: flex-end`). Updated the now-false comment above `sheetPageTitleHtml` that claimed the duplication was intentional.
**AC badge**: `.cs-mi-ac-badge` was `display:inline-flex` with no `flex-direction` set, defaulting to row — "AC" and "14" were laid out side by side despite the comment above the rule correctly describing the intended stacked look. This was a real gap between what a prior session's computed-style check confirmed and what the CSS actually declared — the rule never had `flex-direction:column` to begin with. Fixed: `flex-direction:column; align-items:center; justify-content:center;` plus `line-height:1` on both the label and number spans (their default line-heights were adding visible gap between the two lines that the tight Figma stacking doesn't have). Verified via `get_metadata` on the real Figma node — label and number are true stacked/centered elements, confirming the direction of the fix, not just its visual plausibility.
**HP bar**: was a fixed `120px × 10px` sliver. Pulled `get_design_context` on the real Figma bar node (`55:5981`) — it's `border-radius:999px`, height **37px** (full row height, matching the input boxes beside it), width flexible (fills whatever space is left in a 564px-wide row after the label/inputs/fraction text). Implemented as `.cs-mi-hp-slot` growing (`flex:1 1 320px`, was `0 0 auto`) so `.cs-mi-hp-compact-bar` (now `flex:1 1 auto; height:37px`) has room to expand into. Also fixed a real, separate, lower-priority violation of this file's own CSS rule (§8, "no raw hex outside `:root`") caught while touching this rule anyway — the bar's unfilled-track color was a bare `#371212`; added `--red-track` to `tokens.css` and pointed the rule at it. The fill color (`--red-strong`) was already correct — confirmed it matches the Figma fill hex (`#d62b2b`) exactly, no change needed there.
**Found but deliberately NOT changed, flagging for a DM decision**: the real Figma frame's Temp HP block also has a bar + "/ max" fraction (bicolor, cyan `#0d9ace`/`#042937`, same structure as the HP bar) — but this session's earlier cosmetic pass explicitly built and then *reverted* a Temp HP bar on the DM's own instruction, on the grounds that "5e has no real max temp HP to show a bar or proportion against," landing on a plain number-only badge instead (recorded twice in the Decision Log above). Since there's no `hp_temp_max` column and no data model for it, and the DM's own prior call was deliberate rather than a mistake, I did not reinstate a Temp HP bar just because the Figma frame happens to show one — that would silently reverse a discussed decision rather than execute the batch of fixes actually asked for. Left Temp HP as a plain input, unchanged. Flagging the conflict here rather than picking a side.
**Speaks block**: markup nested the "spoken where" sub-text inside the same flex column as the language name, with the rune glyph as a sibling centered (via `align-items:center`) against that whole two-line column — so the glyph visually landed at the seam between the two lines instead of next to the name, and the sub-text was indented to start after the glyph rather than spanning full width. Restructured to match the real Figma layout: a `.cs-mi-speaks-row` (glyph + name, inline) as one row, `.cs-mi-speaks-sub` as a second, separate, unindented full-width row below it. Also checked whether "Noto Sans Runic" (named in the DM's ask) is loaded anywhere in the codebase — it isn't, in this file or any other (`character.js`/`character.html`/`dm.html`), so there's nothing to "fix" there; it was never wired in. The rune glyphs (e.g. `ᚱᚢᚾ`) do render as real glyph shapes, not tofu/placeholder boxes — confirmed by temporarily blowing the glyph up to 80px in the live DOM and screenshotting it — via the browser's own system-font fallback for the Runic Unicode block, not a font this codebase declares. Left as-is since it's visually correct; noting the "Noto Sans Runic" name doesn't correspond to anything actually in the repo, in case the DM wants that font declared explicitly for consistency across browsers/OSes rather than relying on fallback.
**Tab bar width**: `.cs-tabbar` was rendered as a direct child of `.cs-wrap` (the page's narrow, 820px-capped default container), while literally every other section on the page (MainInfo header, page titles, Who You Are / Your Background / Your Past accordions, Ability Scores cards) already renders inside the wide `.cs-ov-breakout > .cs-ov-inner` wrapper built for the Aug 7 Overview redesign. The tab bar was the one piece never migrated into it, which is why it visibly sat inset ~290px per side relative to everything else at this viewport. Fixed by wrapping `tabBarHtml` in the same breakout markup — no new CSS needed, just adopting the existing pattern.
Verification: full reload cycle (cache-busted query string each time, not relying on HMR) against Kael Evander for all 5 fixes with DOM `getBoundingClientRect()`/`getComputedStyle()` checks (not just visual screenshots) confirming exact pixel results — AC badge `flex-direction:column` confirmed computed, HP bar 557×37px pill confirmed, Speaks row/sub rects confirmed non-overlapping and unindented, tab bar `x:20 w:1360` confirmed matching the Who You Are card's own rect exactly. Spot-checked Visenya Oryn (different species/HP-at-full/multi-glyph Conclave Script language) — bar renders correctly fully green... fully red-filled at 10/10 HP, multi-character glyph row renders correctly. Checked mobile viewport (375px) — HP bar and tab bar both reflow correctly, no overlap or overflow. Zero console errors throughout on both characters, both viewports. Committed but **not yet pushed** — awaiting DM confirmation before it goes live on GitHub Pages.

Heart face...") rather than Kael's flowing prose, not because of a bug but because there's no "YOUR APPEARANCE" block in her data to extract.
